# 0x190000-0x1F0000: notes from the 2026-10-24 matching rounds (NaGa, with Claude lane agents)
Two waves of lane agents worked every function that was still unmatched in this range. Each section below is one agent's notes:
per function the result, what was tried (dead ends included), inferred layouts and names, and the techniques that worked.
The distilled techniques are in `docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md` ("NaGa's lanes"). Drafts are in `build/hand_wip/` as `NAME.laneN.vK.*` (wave 1) and
`NAME.w2laneN.vK.*` (wave 2).

**Held back pending NaGa's ruling:** `build/hand_wip/func_00192BD8.w5lane5.v6.cpp` is byte-identical but writes one store as an
asm `sw` (rule-clean v8 is aligned 1). *(T1834: now in src/hand - func_001E9AC0.cpp already uses the same `sw` StoreX helper.)* **Also held back (padding-style constructs):** `build/hand_wip/func_001AA1D8.w3lane10.v4.cpp` is byte-identical but declares two
empty no-op RAII objects only to shift a stack slot. (func_001A87F0's padded draft was superseded by a natural match in wave 3.)

## Agent lane1

# lane1 notes (agent 1)

## General lessons
- **sched1 "birthing insn" boost (gcc 2.95 haifa-sched adjust_priority/birthing_insn_p).** When an insn becomes ready in the
  pre-reload scheduler and it SETs a register that is live and has REG_N_SETS == 1 *in the whole function* (e.g. $6/$7 when the
  function has only one 3/4-argument call, or a pseudo set once), its priority is raised to max_priority, so it jumps the queue.
  So the order of the arg-setup insns of a call (a0..a3, lui/addiu) depends on how many OTHER places in the function set the same
  argument registers. Adding a dummy call with more args elsewhere in a test copy flips the order; that is how you test it.
  Next tie-breakers (rank_for_schedule): priority, INSN_REG_WEIGHT (smaller wins; `move $a1,pseudo` that kills the pseudo = 0, other
  sets = 1), class vs last scheduled insn, number of dependents (more wins), lowest LUID. sched2 then keeps sched1's order as LUID
  for its ties, and dbr puts the LAST insn of the block into the call's delay slot.
- Use `-da -fsched-verbose-5` and read the "scheduling visualization" table of `.sched` (sched1) and `.sched2` for the block.
- A pseudo set before a call is hoisted by sched1 above the call only if it is not dependent on it; insns after the call in the stream
  get REG_DEP_ANTI on the call.

## func_001ACFE0 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001ACFE0.cpp)
The CRef-call tail (case 34) is exactly the func_0013D328/func_001D54B8 idiom:
`static inline void SetPv(O*po,const CRef&r,void*){CRef arg(r); const CRef &ret=func_00258200(po,arg,D_003D4A60);}` and the call site
`O*po=D_003E0FE4; CRef ref(true); SetPv(po,ref,D_003D4A60);` The call-site context argument is evaluated before the copy ctor call
(and then CSE'd away), the body's own D_003D4A60 lands after it. Every earlier variant ("Send" with a by-value CRef parameter, direct
member call, free function) had the right instructions but a2/a3 setup in the wrong order (2/97) because with a single 3-arg call in the
function the scheduler boosted the a2 and a3-low setters. Using the body's D_003D4A60 (not the unused parameter) is required.

## func_001C9190 - near-miss, 6/147 raw diffs, aligned 5 (build/hand_wip/func_001C9190.lane1.v4.cpp, no pins/asm)
Plain C++ rewrite (no asm, no pins). What got it from 41 aligned to 5:
1. Inner loop written `for(;;){ BeginFrame...; if(status.count!=0)break; body }` (same code as the comma-`while`, but matches the
   147-word length).
2. `do{ D_002DEA4C->flags&=-2; }while(0);` - a do/while(0) adds a loop-depth level, REG_N_REFS weights uses by loop depth, so
   hi(D_002DEA4C) becomes the highest-priority global after the matrix/builder pointers and gets s3 (target order s0 matrix, s1
   builder, s2 hi(BE538), s3 hi(DEA4C), s4 &C10C0, s5 &clock, s6 hi(3A40), s7 hi(BBB60), fp hi(C10C0)). This is global-alloc priority
   = floor_log2(n)*n/live_length with n weighted by loop depth (use in depth-2 loop counts 3, depth-1 counts 2, set counts 1);
   compare numbers in the `.greg` dump ("N regs to allocate: ..." is the priority order).
3. `float t = -*D_002DD314 + clock->frameT;` (not `frameT - *p`): adds one neg insn, which changes the loop size and the
   sched1 order of the first load group; the whole first block then matches.
Remaining 2 groups: (a) `addiu a1,zero,1` (m_fCheckCnt) is rematerialised late into v0 instead of early into a1; (b) the lui for
D_002DEDBC is v0 not v1. Cause (verified in the loop dump): loop.c moves single-use `lui`/const insns out of the inner loop in its
second pass only while `threshold*savings*lifetime >= insn_count` (threshold ~95 here, decreasing by 3 per move; insn_count is the
inner loop size at that moment, 92-94 for my variants). Moved ones become unallocated globals that reload rematerialises into a
round-robin reload register. The target clearly kept const 1 / hi(D_002DD314) / hi(GIFS) local (local-alloc picks a1/a0/v0), i.e. its
inner loop had >= 96 insns at that point (3+ more than mine), probably because gcse PRE did not hoist the single-use `high` insns
(see "PRE/HOIST" lines in the `.gcse` dump). I could not find a natural source change that adds those insns. 144 spelling variants
(search9190.sh style) all stay >= 6.

## func_001BFDB0 - not matched (draft uses 2 pins + asm, rejected by policy); findings
Target keeps vmove copies (vf8 = a, vf7 = c) used only by the inside-triangle block; calls and the 2nd/3rd edge tests use the
originals (vf6, vf5, vf3). gcse COPY-PROP removes plain copies (`.gcse`: "COPY-PROP: Replacing reg..."); only an asm with outputs
or a pinned hard reg keeps them. With an `asm("":"=j"(aa):"0"(a))` copy the code shape matches but global-alloc priority is wrong:
the VU register order in the target is p=vf4, c=vf3, b=vf5, a=vf6, cc=vf7, aa=vf8, ab=vf9, i.e. allocation priority
c,p > b > a > cc > aa > ab; with plain copies (3 and 2 refs) the priorities would come out exactly like that, but any asm copy adds
refs (aa 5, cc 4) and puts them above `a`. Unsolved: what source construct keeps a plain TI-mode copy alive through gcse.

## Agent lane2

# Lane 2 notes (agent N=2)

## func_001A3F78 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001A3F78.cpp, no pins)
Started from build/hand_wip/func_001A3F78.cpp (aligned 17, one $f2 pin). Four independent fixes, in order of effect:
1. `int one = base + 1;` as its own local in the inlined BitIndex (then `result = c ? one : base`) lets loop.c hoist `base+1`
   out of the do-while (`addiu t8,t6,1` before the loop, `daddu v0,t8,zero` inside). With `result = base+1; if(!c) result=base;`
   `result` is set several times so loop.c cannot hoist it. Fixed t8/t9 numbering too. (17 -> 16 but straightened the loop head)
2. `pos` passed as `const qword &` to Length/Score (aggregate address taken) gives it a stack home at 0x40(sp); the per-iteration
   `lqc2` becomes an invariant memory load and loop.c hoists it in front of the inner loop (`lqc2 vf3,0x40(sp)` at the top of
   the `if (word)` block) - this fixed ~60 instructions of schedule (16 -> 6). A pseudo that is merely spilled gets reloaded
   inside the loop and is never hoisted.
3. Reload picks the scratch GPR for an asm `"=r"` float output (qmfc2 temp) and for the block-move scratch (ldl/sdl) with
   `order_regs_for_reload` (non-live call-used regs in numeric order, round-robin over spill_regs). Our 'v0, a1' vs target 'a1, a2'
   was a one-reload shift: writing the *phase-1* qmfc2->mtc1 also as `asm("mtc1 %1,%0" : "=f"(f) : "r"(qword))` (gcc itself emits the
   qmfc2 as an input reload of the "r" qword) makes phase 1 take the first GPR spill reg (v0) and phase 2 then gets a1, a2. 6 -> 0 (with pin).
   The same form also fixed the qmfc2/mtc1/sqc2 order in phase 1 (asm-fed qmfc2 has a latency stall that let the sqc2 slip in).
4. The `register float f asm("$f2")` pin was replaced by one function-scope `float score;` shared by the plane-distance test and
   the ranking loop (it is live across Length's f0/f1 temporaries, so allocation lands on f2). Pin count 0.

General lessons: (a) "qword param that is read inside an inner loop": make the callee take `const qword &` so the load is a hoistable
memory invariant; (b) when scratch registers of reloads are off by one slot, convert an earlier `qmfc2`-in-asm to gcc-emitted
qmfc2 (`mtc1` asm with `"r"(qword)`); (c) sharing a float local between sections reproduces odd FP register picks without pins.

## func_001DDB08 - near miss (best build/hand_wip/func_001DDB08.c, aligned 2, one pin) - still open after ~40 checks
Remaining diff (pinned draft): target schedules `addiu a0,sp,16 ; lw s1 ; daddu a1,sp ; sd ra ; lui s0 ; sw v0 ; addiu s0,s0,lo` (the `lui s0` is
the *last* ALU op before the first jal), we emit `lui s0` right after `sq s1`. Findings:
- sched2 dump: any reader of a callee-saved reg before a call (here the lo_sum `addiu s0,s0`) gets a REG_DEP_ANTI link to the next call, so
  prio(lui)=prio(addiu)+1=prio(call)+2 > prio(addiu a0,sp,16)=prio(call)+1, hence `lui s0` always wins the first ALU slot. This holds with or
  without the pin (/v) and also for the reduced function without the three ifs, so the target's dependence graph must differ somewhere I could not find.
- Natural (no pin) forms always give pv=s0 (local-alloc picks the first callee-saved for the single-block pseudo pv) and the pg address as
  `lui v0 ; addiu s1,v0` (hi/lo only share a register when the address pseudo is block-local, as in the reduced test, or hard-reg pinned).
- The Ghidra/asm structure is CRef ref(0x16,0); copy ctor 1291A8; 258200(&ret, D_003E0FAC, &tmp, &D_003D4A60); three dtors; 3 nullable resets; 165E38(&D_003D4A60).

## func_001E9858 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001E9858.cpp, no pins; asm = VU0 macro + MMI only)
Started from build/hand_wip/func_001E9858.codex18v2.cpp (aligned 10 but 7 pins + instruction asm). What each fix was:
1. The codex "WORK" struct fields `axis`, `nextEntry`, `world` are NOT locals-in-a-struct. In the target they are spilled pseudos:
   stack slots 0x8C0/0x8D0/0x8E0 sit *after* the real locals (distance at 0x8B0) in allocation order axis, next, world. Use plain locals
   (`qword axis = func_001A6318(p)->c.q; int next = j+1; qword world = Point(...)`), keep WORK = {phase, direction, basis, temporary, inverse, distance}.
   Spilling gives `lq v0,0x20(v0) ; sq v0,0x8C0(sp)` (reload scratch = first free reg v0) - no barrier/ordering asm needed any more.
2. TransposeInto: a single asm with 7 pinned outputs -> two asm statements (pextlw/pextuw with 4 early-clobber temps, then pcpyld/pcpyud with
   3 early-clobber outputs). Temps now allocate before the inputs (v0,v1,a0,a1 vs inputs a2,a3,t0) as in the target, outputs t1,t2,t3.
3. `&phase->hit` read through the struct (`w.phase.hit`) instead of the pointer: fewer refs of the `&w.phase` pseudo, which then lands in s7
   by itself (global-alloc priority = floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length; s-regs are handed out in that order).
4. node vs k s4/s5 tie was priority 0.720 vs 0.726; the changes above shifted lengths by one and the order flipped to the target's.
General: for global-alloc ties, compute the priorities from the *.lreg dump ("used N times across M insns") - a script is easy
(floor_log2(N)*N/M, higher gets the lower-numbered callee-saved reg) and predicts the allocation exactly.

## func_00194F70 - near miss (build/hand_wip/func_00194F70.lane2.v1.cpp, aligned 40 (was 51), NO asm and NO pins)
Base = codex18v1 structure (goto-into-the-middle do-while for the entry search so loop.c leaves it alone; WORK{bounds,point,subject,owned}; n is
decremented) with every instruction-asm helper replaced by plain C. All control flow, frame (0xE0) and the pq/owned stack slots (0x20/0x24)
match. What remains is ONE register-priority tie: the target has i in s1 (and fWrap shares s1 because i is dead in the found path), `a` (entry
pointer) in s2; ours has a in s1, i in s2. Global-alloc priorities from the .lreg dump: a = 5*44/121 = 1.82, i = 3*12/30 = 1.20, so a wins.
To flip it i needs refs>=16 (floor_log2 jumps to 4: 4*16/30=2.13) or a needs refs<=31 or live length >183.
- Tried (no effect or worse): decl order of i,j,e,a; e/a block scoped (the goto then crosses an init); `while(--j>=0)` instead of the goto (loop.c strength-reduces
  it, 117); explicit `i--` in both tails (56); pin on a (94) or i (54: a pinned reg cannot be shared with fWrap, frame grows); a ae[j]-direct (92).
- A bug-version where the second loop's `a->x0` kept the old `a` live (refs 41, len 205 -> 1.0) gave aligned 20, so the target's `a` has a long live
  range or ~30 refs; I could not find a natural source for that. A separate `src` pointer for the second loop gives a: refs 36/len 109 (1.65), still too high.

## func_00194A40 - near miss (existing build/hand_wip/func_00194A40.cpp, aligned 20, one $3 pin) - unchanged
Nine of the diffs are the same swap in every Insert() call setup: target `daddu a3,s0,zero ; movz t0,zero,s0`, ours `movz t0,zero,s0 ; daddu a3,s0,zero`.
sched1 ranks the cmove (the null-checked derived->base conversion, REG_DEAD on the pre-add pseudo => INSN_REG_WEIGHT 0) ahead of the plain a3 move
(weight 1), and sched2 keeps sched1's order for ties (lowest luid first). The target needs weight(a3 move) <= weight(cmove), e.g. a3's source dying
at that move; plain `void *object = n` is copy-propagated by cse (loop var has the lower pseudo number). An empty asm copy
(`asm("" : "=r"(object) : "0"(n))`) reproduces the order but costs a slot (aligned 21, new diffs). Not solved.

## func_0019CD38 - analysed, not matched (existing build/hand_wip/func_0019CD38.cpp, aligned 105)
Register-agnostic diff (build/lane_scratch/lane2/structdiff.py: compile, wildcard registers/immediates, difflib) shows the existing draft is structurally
almost right: only ~10 real differences; the 105 aligned diffs are a one-register shift (ours uses s0..s6, target s0..s5, frame 0x90 vs 0x80).
Cause found: `&p->sub` (p+0x24, used by func_0019DCA0 and func_0019D660 in the aux block) is hoisted by sched1 to the top of the block (pseudo, no deps),
so it overlaps the zero-vector address pseudo (addiu s0,s5,lo) and needs its own callee-saved register (s1) and pushes aux to s2.
In the target both live in s0 (sub is computed *after* the two SetVector calls: `addiu s0,s2,0x24 ; lw a1,0x44(s2) ; jal 19DCA0`) and aux is in s1 (=next's reg,
next is dead after the switch dispatch). Other target facts: s4 = hi(D_002A2DD0) and s5 = hi(D_002DDF40) are the two gcse "high" pseudos (copies `daddu s5,s0,zero`
at the end of each path), effect = s3, this = s2, next = s1. Not found: a source form that keeps `p+0x24` from being scheduled early (tried: none yet).
## func_001D2610 / func_001E8378 - not reached.

## Tools I wrote (in build/lane_scratch/lane2/, not under tools/)
- prio.py / evalv.sh: from an -da dump (.lreg/.greg) print floor_log2(refs)*refs/len per pseudo and its allocated hard reg (predicts global-alloc order exactly).
- structdiff.py SRC FUNC: register/immediate-agnostic aligned diff against the target (shows real structural differences when a register shift makes the plain diff useless).

## Agent lane3

# Lane 3 notes

## func_0019AC88 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_0019AC88.c)
No pins. Fix was `float h = t*0.5f; g *= h;` instead of `t *= 0.5f; g *= t;`.
Why: sched1 `rank_for_schedule` breaks ties (priority, reg-weight) by the number of *forward dependences* (INSN_DEPEND count),
then by LUID. Overwriting `t` made the `f13 = t` argument move (insn 71) anti-dependent on the later `t = t*0.5` insn, so it had one
more forward dep than the por a1/a2 moves, ranked above them and was emitted before them (so the por landed in the jal delay slot).
Writing the result to a new local removes that edge, f13 ties with a1/a2, loses on LUID and goes last (= delay slot), like the target.
General trick: when an argument move lands in the wrong place relative to other argument moves, look at the -fsched-verbose-5 dump table
("dep"/forward list); extra forward edges (anti deps from later redefinition of the same variable) raise an insn's rank.
Do not reuse/overwrite a parameter variable after the call if the target doesn't.

## func_001A4AD8 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001A4AD8.cpp)
Started from v3 (aligned 6). Everything was scheduling-barrier / stream-order, no pins needed. Steps:
1. Copy macro: the old `do{...}while(0)` around everything made the loop-begin NOTE a scheduling barrier right before `d_=D`, so the
   d copy / s_+0x120 / `i+1` / counter init were forced after the m_pb store (aligned 6). `{ }` instead (no barrier) lets them float
   too early (16). The target needs d_/s_ initialised BEFORE the barrier (they are hoisted/free) and `i+1`/counter after it:
   `uint*d_=(D);const uint*s_=(S); do{ int k; for(k=0;k<12;k+=4){...; s_+=4; d_+=4;} }while(0);` (2 diffs).
   NOTE: use an UP-counting k (k=0;k<12;k+=4) with explicit s_+=4/d_+=4: loop.c reverses it itself (counter 8, bgez) and the
   reversed-counter init insn is created after gcse, so it ends up after the PRE-inserted `i+1` in the stream (sched keeps that
   order). An explicit down-counting `j=8; j>=0; j-=4` gave a3=8 before t1=i+1 (wrong order).
2. Second loop's `lui/addiu` hoists: a separate `const uint *src = D_002BFC60.words;` inside the loop body makes loop.c hoist the
   whole address (lui+addiu) and `daddu a3,t4,zero` copy in-loop (target); using the global directly only hoisted the lui.
3. Final 2 diffs were the order of hoisted lui's (D_002DEA28, D_002BFC60, 0x6C03 tag const): loop.c hoists in program order, sched
   ties on LUID. Declaring `VIFS *g = &D_002DEA28; const uint *src = ...;` first in the body and passing the tag inline
   `Write(g, 0x6C03C000|(i*3+0x15))` gives [2E,2C,6C03] like the target.
General: a `do{}while(0)` macro (or any loop) inserts NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG, which in sched is a barrier: the first insn after it depends
on everything before and everything after depends on it. Where you put the note relative to the setup insns decides which insns can
float. -fsched-verbose-5 visualization tables (per block) show it clearly.

## func_001DB0C8 - near-miss (parked), build/hand_wip/func_001DB0C8.lane3.v1.cpp (aligned 28, policy-clean; old draft's 24 uses pins + lw/addiu instruction asm)
Findings (all verified by experiment):
- The old draft's MissingAndTime/Scalar `lw`/`addiu` instruction-asm + two pins are not needed for the data flow: `*(const int*)&f` as the "r" operand of
  qmtc2 gives the target's `lw v0,0x3274(v0); qmtc2.ni v0,vf2` (the func_0011F960 VU_FLOAT_V idiom). `p->changed=D_002DDF0C; if(mode==-1||p->selected==-1)return;`
  is the natural top. Remaining top diff: target has `addiu v1,-1` BEFORE the lwc1 (v1 vs our v0): sched1 puts lwc1 first (prio 4 vs 2) and the -1 then
  shares v0. `int none=-1` before the first compare gives the target's v1 but hoists the addiu into the first block (also wrong).
- VF allocation: `ray` must be a plain `qword` pseudo live across the loop calls (spilled by reload to 0x80, def `vmulx.xyzw vf3,vf1,vf2x` gets vf3 from reload),
  NOT a union/struct home. With ray as a `U` the vmulx dest was vf1 and origin got vf3.
- `origin` (VF pseudo crossing the 2 loop calls) is only kept in a VF reg with caller-save sqc2/lqc2 around the calls when REG_N_REFS > 4*calls crossed
  (gcc CALLER_SAVE_PROFITABLE). Plain code gives refs=7 (spilled; the call arg then becomes `lq a1,slot`). A second, unused `"j"(a)` operand on the Add asm
  (allocation only) raises it to 9 and gives the target shape, but origin lands in vf3 (target vf1) and the caller-save slot is 0xA0 (target 0x90, shared
  with nothing else). The target's Add results are vf2/vf3 and equal to the ray reload reg (vadd.xyzw vf2,vf1,vf2), ours vf1 (local-alloc takes the lowest free
  reg for the block-local Add result before global-alloc places origin). Not solved.
- Stack slots: 0x00 shared temp home (first declared 16-byte object), hit 0x10, prox 0x70, then reload spill slots ray 0x80, origin(caller-save) 0x90.
  A struct-returning Pack() (VUV) makes the temp slot land after `hit` (0x60) unless it is declared first; the `U home` out-param version keeps 0x00.
- (later) best policy-clean state: build/hand_wip/func_001DB0C8.lane3.v2.cpp (volatile Add asm => no CSE of the two Add(origin,ray), caller-save slot 0x90 and frame 0x150 correct;
  ray as union memory + Scale "=&j" + extra "j"(s) allocation-only operand gives ray=vf3 / scalar=vf2 like the target (that file: w2 in scratch, not saved)).
  Still wrong: origin lives in vf2/vf3 instead of vf1 and the Add results/ray reload regs follow (target: origin vf1; first Add vf2, else-branch Add vf3, both = ray reload reg);
  a `register qword originVF asm("vf1")` pin breaks caller-save (pinned var is recomputed per iteration). Also `addiu v1,-1` order at the top and pack/vadd order.
- func_001DB0C8 more: the target's `qmtc2.ni a1,vf1` (origin to VF) sits in the loop PREHEADER after the count guard (`blez`), so the source computes `ToVF(po)` INSIDE the
  loop body (non-volatile asm with invariant inputs is hoisted by loop.c); `origin=ToVF(origin)` before the loop is wrong. build/hand_wip/func_001DB0C8.lane3.v3.cpp/(k1 in scratch)
  has that and ray as union memory. Unresolved: VF numbering in the loop (origin/b/r take vf2/vf1/vf3 instead of vf1/vf2/vf2; hit by local-alloc giving the Add result vf3).

## func_001BFF28 - near-miss, build/hand_wip/func_001BFF28.lane3.v1.cpp (26/233 differ, aligned 23; old best 67; uses 2 allocation/barrier asms, 0 pins)
Fresh rewrite (route builder: nav-graph path between two points). Layout facts, all verified:
- Stack: MARK object at 0x00 (ctor func_1AE478 / dtor func_1AE4A0(,2), declared inside the else-branch), REQUEST temporary at 0x10
  (`REQ().Set(n)` returning `this` with a user ctor `REQ(){}` so g++ does not zero it; the `this`-returning Set() is what makes the align store go
  through the CSE'd `s0=&req` register while the bytes store stays sp-relative), `V initial` 0x20, `V goal` 0x40, `int found` 0x60,
  reload spill slots 0x64 (param capacity) and 0x68 (write). `write` and `capacity` are plain pseudos that get spilled (low priority);
  the dedupe loop uses separate `rd`/`wr` pointer copies initialised from `write` (otherwise `write` gets an s-reg and shifts everything).
- 9 saved regs: from s0,to s1,start s2,finish s3,first s4,out s5,g s6,last s7,path fp: this order is gcc's global-alloc priority order
  (floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length). out and first are nearly TIED (13*3/176 vs 5*2/45); a 1-insn change in the function flips them.
- Loop 1/2 branch tails (`bnez/bgezl` with the next iteration's first load in the delay slot): the target's last insn before the branch is an
  asm insn (dbr `stop_search_p` stops at asm), so no insn is taken "from before". Emulated with a non-volatile empty `asm("" : "+m"(e->cost))`
  after the cost store (a volatile one blocks the scheduler and pointer increments end up after it).
- PRE (gcse) hoists `qmtc2` asms and `&local` address computations to the loop preheader. The addresses are hoisted in the target too, but the
  `qmtc2 finish` is NOT hoisted above loop 1: a second dummy output on the ToVF asm (not single_set) stops PRE from touching it.
- A named `V *pg = &goal;` used by loop 2 and by the call avoids a hoisted copy (t1=a2) in loop 2.
- `count = min(found, capacity)`: `int n = found; if (n > capacity) n = capacity; count = n;` gives the target's slt/daddu/movz sequence
  (`?:` gets folded to MIN_EXPR -> movn form; `count = capacity; if (...) count = found;` coalesces the reload into s0).
- cost store is `sw` of the qmfc2 GPR: EDGE.cost is an int bits field filled from `"=r"(int)` qmfc2 asm.
Remaining diffs (26): (1) register names in loop 1/2 and the tail: local-alloc priority picks cost->v0, slti->a1 where the target has
slti->v0, cost->a0 (target must have priority(slti) >= priority(cost); mine has slti early in sched1 so its live range is long);

## func_001DF0A0 (in progress; policy-clean best = aligned 8, see build/hand_wip/func_001DF0A0.lane4.v1.c)
The old func_001DF0A0.c (aligned 3) is NOT policy-clean (instruction asm for mult/la/daddu/lw/mtc1, 8+ pins). A clean rewrite
needs only ONE pin, so these are the findings (all verified with -da/-dL dumps):

- **Why the target keeps `mult` (i*0x150) inside the loop (no strength reduction)**: loop.c runs twice (-frerun-loop-opt). The
  constant `0x150` is only hoisted/becomes a giv multiplier when `life*threshold*savings >= insn_count`, with life=1,savings=1 and
  threshold = 95 here (1 + n_non_fixed_regs, loop has a call). So whenever the loop has >= 96 real insns in the 2nd pass
  (>= ~102 in the 1st; the dump line "Loop from X to Y: N real insns" shows it) the constant stays in the loop, i*0x150 is not
  a giv and no reduction happens. Our compact C has 82/74, one-VU-op-per-asm has 96/90, so the original had >20 more invisible
  RTL insns than any natural form I could build. Cheap workaround that is policy-legal: ONE pin `register int stride asm("$2") = sizeof(E);`
  (hard reg operand of the mult hides it from loop.c). Outer loops that contain a real inner loop are never strength reduced
  either (no biv analysis at all) - that is how func_001DEEA8 matched; here there is no inner loop.
- With only that pin, plain C gives all s-regs right (s2=p s5=q s4=i s3=e s1=f s0=so s6=&G s7=hi(D_002A7AC0)), the stack layout
  (`VECTOR pos` union declared before `HIT hit`) and the call setup.
- VU part: use `$vf3` hard-coded inside the dot asm with clobber "vf3" (target skips vf3: d=vf1,end=vf2,start=vf4,pos=vf5), asm
  statements NON-volatile (volatile asm are full scheduling barriers). Fused Len (dot+sqrt in one asm) gives the right VF register
  assignment; split Len gives the target ORDER (B1, A2, S1, B2, qmfc2, S2) but the wrong registers (priority = log2(refs)*refs/life).
- **int bits arg**: target does `qmfc2.ni v0,vf1 ... daddu t2,v0,zero`. Reproduced by declaring the 7th param of func_001A7C18 as
  `long` and passing `(long)lenQword` (plain TImode->DImode subreg; reload emits qmfc2 + daddu). A `int`+asm qmfc2 is combined
  into the arg register (no copy), which also changes the sched2 priorities (the copy makes B1's chain 1 longer).
- Open: prologue `daddu s5,a1` vs `sq s4` order (tie in sched2, prio 5/5, tie broken by LUID), v0/v1 swap between &e->head and the
  qmfc2 scratch, and the order of A2 (vsub dir=end-start) relative to the first sqrt.

## General lessons (lane 4)
1. Strip codex-draft hacks ONE at a time; `0.0f`, ternaries, and pointer locals frequently replace the asm.
2. loop.c hoists constants/invariants only if life*threshold*savings >= insn_count (threshold 95 here); read "Loop from..: N real insns"
   and "move-insn savings .. not desirable" with -da -dL; two loop passes run, so check both counts.
3. Scalar locals whose address is taken (not struct members) are not invalidated by stores through other pointers; struct members are.
4. gcse "PRE/HOIST" (see .gcse dump) creates long-lived address pseudos (e.g. &key) that compete in global alloc; priority is
   floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_len (integer-truncated *10000), ties/near-ties decide s-register order - tune with +-1 insn.
5. Hit/loop-carried copies like `daddu s7,s0,zero` come from loop.c hoisting an address computed INSIDE the loop and cse2 turning it into a copy.

## func_001EA000 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001EA000.c)
Started from func_001EA000.v2.c (aligned 5). Two independent fixes:
1. First wrap-around access `pseg[0].a` was folded by cse1 into `0x10($s3)` because `pseg = ps->aseg10` sat in the same
   extended basic block. Assign `pseg = ps->aseg10;` (function-scope local) after the copy loops, just before the
   func_0014C9F8 call -> aligned 0, only the spill-slot swap (0xC1C/0xC20) left.
2. The swap = order of PRE reaching registers (&ax[0] = reg 312, aseg10 copy = reg 315), which gcse numbers by expression
   hash-bucket order (`hash % table_size`; table size = (n_insns/2)|1 with n_insns = insns at gcse time). reload gives
   the spilled pseudos slots in ascending regno. Adding a dead `nIter++` in the main loop (survives cse1, deleted later
   by flow) bumps the table 101 -> 103 buckets, flips the order, and the whole function matches.
General trick: when two spill slots / PRE-created pseudos come out swapped, the cause is the gcse expression hash table
size; any dead-but-not-cse-dead code (self-referencing counter) that adds 1-4 insns can reorder them. Check
`Expression hash table (N buckets` in the -da .gcse dump.

## Lessons (lane 5, consolidated)
1. gcse expression hash table size ((n_insns/2)|1 after cse1) decides PRE reaching-reg numbering and hence spill-slot order; a dead counter that
   survives cse1 (needs a call in the loop) can flip it (func_001EA000).
2. A volatile earlyclobber asm (sqrt.s) acts as a full scheduler barrier; a user variable assigned in two basic blocks becomes one big
   quantity and changes local-alloc priority - split it (func_0019A750).
3. A `"rV"` memory operand makes reload emit `lw rX,off(rX)`; reaching through a pointer variable (`M4 *pr=&local`) reproduces the target's mixed
   sp/reg addressing because cse1 folds `(mem (reg X))` but not `(mem (plus X c))` (func_001B20B8, func_001B72B0).
4. For VU code the vf register order is local-alloc priority; duplicate allocation-only "j" operands (commented) are the pin-free way to steer it.

## func_00196928 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_00196928.c)
- The old .c draft (aligned 4) differed only in the schedule of the incoming-v home store (`sq a0,0x20(sp)`) and `daddu a0,sp`.
  Cause: the draft read v through an explicit `lqc2 %1,%2` with an "m"(*pv) operand, so v had a real memory home.
  In the target the `sq` is a *spill store* and the `lqc2 vf2,0x20(sp)` is a *reload*: v is a TImode pseudo whose class is VU ("j"), spilled across the two calls.
- Fix: LenXY takes `qword v` by value, `vmul.xy %0,%1,%1; vaddy.x ...` uses `"j"(v)`. ONE "j"(v) keeps v in $s1 (a qmtc2 is emitted);
  a SECOND unused `"j"(v)` operand (allocation-only, same trick as func_0011EFB0) pushes the class preference to VU and v is spilled.
- Plain C `a<b?b:a` gives `max.s fd,fs,ft` in the target operand order (`a>b?a:b` gives the swapped order), so no max asm is needed. sqrt must stay the
  `"=&f"` sqrt.s asm (target has `sqrt.s f2,f0; mov.s f0,f2`).
- Lesson: a by-value qword param that feeds VU0 asm and is live across calls must not get an "m" operand; give it "j" inputs and let gcc reload it with `lqc2`
  (add a duplicate unused "j" input if it ends up in a callee-saved GPR instead).

## func_001C3500 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001C3500.cpp), zero pins
- Old codex drafts used ~24 vf/GPR pins; the match is a fresh pin-free rewrite with the project's VU asm helper idiom: Length/Unit/Normalize(v,fallback)/
  Sub/Dot/Transform/Cross each as its own asm with "j" operands, `X(len)` = mtc1 asm on the qmfc2'd length, `Normalize` = `if(X(len)<1e-4f) return fallback; return Unit(v,len)`.
  Going 27 -> 0 took: natural helpers, `rot=func_0011BA08(..)` instead of a "temporary" field (gcc's own 0xB0 call temp + lq/sq copy), earlyclobber `"=&j"` on the
  Transform asm so the result does not share row0's register, then the stack-slot trick below.
- The fallback global is passed by value (eager `lq`); `float r=50.0f` declared inside the loop gives the callee-saved f20.
- `WORK{M3 basis; tangent, first, direction}` only fixes the stack layout.
- Final negate = `VU_FLOAT(-1.0f)*VU_VECTOR(row)` for rows[1] first, then rows[2] (VU_FLOAT(float) = qmtc2 asm ctor, as in func_00151700). The two temporaries CSE to one qmtc2
  and one dead `sqc2` temp store (the by-value VU_FLOAT param copy). Swapping the statement order swaps the vf registers.
- GENERAL TRICK (stack temp slot sharing): that dead temp store has to land in the slot of `back` (0x70). Declaring `back` (a union U, so it is forced to memory at its
  declaration) INSIDE an inner block makes its slot a freed temp slot when the block ends; the next 16-byte C++ temporary (the VU_FLOAT) reuses it. `fwd` must be declared
  OUTSIDE that block (if both are inside, the older one, fwd, is picked first). Scalar TI locals whose address is taken get their slot lazily at the END of the frame; unions/structs
  get it at declaration, in ascending address order (MIPS: first declared = lowest offset). Freed temp slots are reused best-fit.

## func_001E95B0 - near-miss: 11/157 literal, aligned 11, ZERO pins (build/hand_wip/func_001E95B0.lane6.v2.cpp)
- The old drafts used 2 asm pins + asm `lw` hacks + vf2/vf1 pins; none are needed. Natural form:
  * `VU_FLOAT(float)` (qmtc2 asm ctor) / `VU_VECTOR(const qword&)` / `VecCombo(f1,v1,f2,v2)` = vmulax/vmaddx (project idiom, see func_00151700). Passing VU_FLOAT by
    value makes gcc store the vf regs to the same stack slot (`sqc2 vf3,0(sp)`) = the target's dead scratch stores.
  * `static inline void Lerp(U*pd,const U*p0,const U*p1,float f,float cf){pd->q=VecCombo(VU_FLOAT(cf),VU_VECTOR(p0->q),VU_FLOAT(f),VU_VECTOR(p1->q));}` called as
    Lerp(&a[n-2],&a[n-3],&a[n-1],f,cf) for a, b, path->pos. Gives the three pointer pseudos (user vars) and the `daddu` copies via reload_cse. Direct member args instead
    of the pointer helper = 30 diffs; pointer helper = 15.
  * Reverse branch `p->path->count=i+1` (NOT a local `path`): gcse leaves the `daddu a1,a2` copy that the target has, so no register pin is needed.
  * A duplicated allocation-only operand `"j"(v2.q), "j"(v2.q)` on the VecCombo asm (raises v2's local-alloc priority) makes all three vf register sets match.
- Only diff left (11 words): the n*4 pseudo gets $a2 and the right-pointer copy $a3; target has n*4=$a3, pointer=$a2. Model: local-alloc priority =
  floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/(death-birth). n*4 = 7 refs over ~82 insns = .1707; each pointer pseudo = 2 refs over 13 insns = .1538, so n*4 is allocated first. Verified by experiment: removing ONE
  n*4 use makes the block allocate exactly like the target. So the target's n*4 has lower priority (refs 6 or lifetime >= ~92 insns) or the pointers a higher one.
  Not found: Lerp param permutations, `U*pa=p->a` local bases, ++n / n+=1 / 1.0f / inline cf / dist expression swap, empty memory-clobber asm (schedule much worse), extra "r"/"m" asm
  operands on the pointers (adds instructions), a register pin on n*4 (a hard-reg operand is put second in `addu`, X then no longer reuses a3), a pin on Q (copy-propagated away).
  Next ideas: make n*4 a global pseudo (live across blocks) or shave >=2 insns from the pointer lifetimes in the sched1 order.

## Reusable lessons
1. Fresh pin-free rewrite with one asm per VU op beats old pinned drafts: pins fought the allocator (func_001C3500 27 -> 0, func_001E95B0 15 -> 11, func_00196928 4 -> 0).
2. Earlyclobber `"=&j"` on a multi-instruction asm (matrix transform) stops the output sharing an input register; a duplicated unused "j" input raises a quantity's local-alloc priority.
3. Stack temps: aggregates declared in an inner block free their slot at block end and the next same-size C++ temporary reuses it (best fit); use that to place dead temp stores.
4. local-alloc order = priority floor_log2(refs)*refs/(death-birth), global pseudos are allocated AFTER all block-local ones; `.lreg` dump shows refs/lifetime per pseudo and the hard reg chosen.

### func_001CDF88: layout and what mattered
Frame 0xA0: DLI at 0x00 {pdl,ppv,ibDle,pdliNext}, lo 0x10, hi 0x20, t 0x30 (grow vector, later reused for the midpoint), half 0x40.
Object: time 0x14, child corner vectors 0x70/0x80, vtable 0xEC (child update = slot 4), size 0x154, child DL list 0x15C, lo/hi/center
0x170/0x180/0x190, radius 0x1A0. g_pdliFirst = D_002DD2FC, clock = D_002DDED0.
1. `lo`/`hi` init: write .y/.z through POINTER variables (`union U *plo=&lo; plo->f[1]=plo->f[2]=F; lo.f[0]=F;`). The pointer store makes cse
   re-read dli.ppv/ibDle from the stack (target does `lw a0,4(sp)` again) and gives `addiu a3,sp,0x10; swc1 4(a3); swc1 8(a3); swc1 0(sp)`. 17 -> 5.
2. `extern const float D_002A77D0, D_002A77D4;` (rodata FLT_MAX constants): without const the alias analysis orders the hi load before the lo.y/z stores
   and the prologue order is wrong.
3. Chained fill `t.f[0]=t.f[1]=t.f[2]=s` gives the target store order x,z,y.
4. `float h = 0.5f` passed to the qmtc2 asm as "r"(h) gives lui/mtc1/mfc1/qmtc2 like the target (an int constant gives lui $v0 directly).
5. VF numbers come from local-alloc priority (log2(refs)*refs/life, lowest free vf first); a duplicate allocation-only `"j"(k)` operand on the Mid asm
   gives k the 4 references that put it in vf2 (otherwise vf4).
6. Length = two asm statements (vmul/vaddw/vadday/vmaddz ; vsqrt/vwaitq/vaddq) + a separate qmfc2 asm; Sub stays its own asm so the `one` scratch can share vf2 with k.
7. The final order lo-store / t-store / hi-store / sw radius / center-in-jal-delay-slot could not be reached by statement reordering (720 permutations):
   it matched when the three Length asms carry `"memory"` clobbers and the stores are written in the target order between them (lo between the two Length
   steps, t after the second, hi/radius/center after qmfc2), with `lv=lo.q, hv=hi.q` copied into locals first so the barrier does not force reloads.
Dead ends: volatile asm, empty "+j"(mid) dependency asm, plain-C qmfc2 via `(int)qword` (dsll32/dsra32), a pin on `one`, a "vf2" clobber.

### func_001CFE88: what mattered
The old codex draft had 4 VF pins and an `lq` asm (13 aligned). Without them (clean) 23. The winning changes:
- GPR->VU copy of the velocity qword as a PLAIN C move (`qword v=p->vel; Blend(delta,v,...)`, no ToVu asm): gcc then emits `lq a1` + `qmtc2.ni a1,vf8` itself.
  Replacing the ToVu asm took aligned 14 -> 5, then a statement-order search (105 orders) found 0 (order: delta, w1, w2, w3, dt, cdt, v).
- Blend with a fresh output (`"=j"(r)`, v input only) so the velocity vector has only 2 references and gets vf8.
- Splat helper = asm qmtc2 into a local + union store (the dead `sqc2 ...,0x70(sp)` stores).
- Matrix Mul rows: outputs "=j" with the b row as ordinary input (no "0" matching constraint needed).

## General lessons (reusable)
1. A near-miss draft full of instruction-level asm (lw/lui/swc1/lq ...) is often WORSE than natural C once the asm is replaced by single VU-op asms; strip the asm first
   (001B1328 went 5 -> 3 -> 2 -> 0 and 001CFE88 13 -> 0 that way). GPR<->VU qword copies should be plain C moves (gcc emits qmtc2/qmfc2 itself, and can fill delay slots with them).
2. `"memory"` clobbers on the VU asm statements are a real ordering tool: loads/stores written between two clobbered asms stay between them (explains stores that sit in the middle
   of a vsqrt/vaddq sequence). Copy values into locals first or the barrier forces cse to reload them.
3. Earlyclobber outputs (`"=&j"`) also conflict with registers that died in the IMMEDIATELY preceding insn (local-alloc numbering): a scratch that should share a register with a value
   used just before needs one insn in between (here: keep Sub as its own asm).
4. Local-alloc priority is log2(refs)*refs/life; an allocation-only duplicate operand ("j"(k) twice) moves a value up in vf numbering; a value that is only read once (not "+j") gets
   fewer refs and a higher vf number. Local pseudos are allocated before global ones; a scratch with no later use takes vf1 unless a pin on the long-lived value prevents it.
5. `extern const float` for constants in .rodata changes alias analysis in sched1/sched2 and can fix a prologue order.
6. Partial vector init through a pointer variable (`U *p=&v; p->f[1]=p->f[2]=x; v.f[0]=x;`) reproduces `addiu a3,sp,16; swc1 4(a3); swc1 8(a3); swc1 0(sp)`.
7. Once the asm is clean, a permutation search over statement order (105-720 compiles, in-process via matchtool.compile_src/compare) finds orders no one would guess.
8. Index arithmetic `p + n*12 (+disp)` that ends as separate `mult`/`addu` + a pointer copy needs the table as a real struct array member (`p->tab[n].field`), not casts.
9. gcc fuses `(a*b)+c` into the R5900 `*mul_acc_si_r5900` insn when the product has one use; a target showing separate mult/addu means the product/address came from an array member expansion.

# Lane 8 notes (agent 8)

## func_001E9C88  (near-miss; not matched yet)
Target structure (all confirmed by compiling): loop `for (; t < tEnd; t += D_002C367C)` sampling a path; unique-insert of
`pCur` into `apv[64]` (search loop `for i<cpv`, then `if (i >= cpv && (unsigned)cpv < 64) apv[cpv++] = pCur`),
segment bookkeeping in `aseg[64]` (pseg/cseg), then copy to heap.  Registers: s0 pCur, s1 outside, s2 pPrev, s3 ps, s4 pseg,
s5 cpv, s6 ppv (strength-reduced `&apv[cpv]`), s7 cseg, fp hoisted `&pos2` (gcse), a3 spilled `lui` of D_002C367C.

Old draft `func_001E9C88.c` (aligned 4) has TWO pins (`cpv $21`, `ppv $22`); the `cpv` pin is unnecessary (aligned stays 4
with only the `ppv` pin), but the `ppv` pin keeps `addiu s6,sp,0x200` BEFORE the loop-entry `bc1f`; target has it AFTER (loop
preheader).  That also explains the target's prologue order (sq s6 last: no WAR successor in the first block).

What the target really is: ppv is NOT a source variable; it is the loop.c giv for `apv[cpv++] = ...` (init emitted in the
preheader after the entry test).  Discoveries (zero pins):
* gcse (M-R "PRE/HOIST") hoists ANY lone `sp+K` computed after a loop (here `&apv` for the final call, and `&pos2`) to the end of
  the first block.  `&pos2` gets fp.  The hoisted `&apv` pseudo P would have to be spilled (frame grows 0x3F0 -> 0x400, `sw v1,0x320(sp)`)
  unless it gets a REG_EQUIV: that happens iff an EARLIER pseudo holding `sp+0x200` exists when cse2 runs (cse2 rewrites
  `P = sp+512` to `P = q` with REG_EQUAL, update_equiv_regs then makes it an equivalence and reload rematerializes `addiu`).
  Recipe that gives exactly the target's code shape: declare `void **q = apv;` at the very top (before the first call; if it is
  assigned right before the loop, combine merges it and the trick breaks) and use ONLY q for the store: `q[cpv++] = pCur;`
  (A = search loop and C = final call keep using `apv`).  Result: `build/hand_wip/func_001E9C88.lane8.v1.c`, 0 pins, aligned 22 but
  ALL remaining diffs are the s6/s7/fp rotation (ours: cseg s6, &pos2 s7, ppv fp; target: ppv s6, cseg s7, &pos2 fp), the
  matching prologue order and one operand.  Code, frame (0x3F0), loop layout, spill of a3 and init position are all right.
* Why the rotation: global-alloc order is by floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length.  ppv: 7 refs, live length 118 (!), cseg .193,
  &pos2 .132, ppv .119.  The target needs ppv > cseg > &pos2.  The length of ppv is DOUBLED (59 -> 118) at the local-alloc dump
  whenever loop.c's init is a plain copy of a pseudo (`giv = q` / `giv = P`) and the store precedes the increment (store-first).
  With increment-first (`cpv++; q[cpv-1] = pCur;`) the init is `sp+0x1FC` (not a copy), length stays 59 and the allocation matches the
  target exactly (aligned 2: only `addiu s6,0x1FC` init and `addiu/sw` order).  I could not find a source form that has the
  init = `sp+0x200` constant (cse2 always canonicalizes it to a copy of P) without the doubling.
* Single pin experiments: pinning cseg $23 gives aligned 13 (&pos2 gets s6, ppv fp: &pos2 .132 > ppv .119).  Pinning &pos2 or ppv
  is impossible/useless.  Guard forms (`if (t<tEnd){ w=apv; loop }`), explicit pointer `*w++`, pinned `w`, u-forms: all worse (31-72).
* Dead ends: inline helper `Addr(apv)` for A/B/C (no effect), `aseg`-relative index forms, B1 `(char*)aseg+512+cpv*4`, all the
  `int k = cpv++; q[k]=...` variants (init 0x1FC, store after incr), q placed after the loop-entry calls (combine merges it), `i == cpv`,
  `sizeof` cap, `64u` (identical), while/for/do forms, pinned q.
General lessons: (1) a stack array whose address is formed at several places is hoisted by gcse and spilled unless an early
pseudo copy exists (cse2 turns the hoisted insn into a copy with REG_EQUAL -> reload rematerializes); (2) `-da` order list
"N regs to allocate: ..." in the .greg dump shows the exact global-alloc priority order; compare it with the target's register
assignment to see which pseudo must be more important.

### func_001E9C88 addendum (more experiments)
* Why the ppv live length doubles (118 vs 59): the SECOND loop pass (-frerun-loop-opt) re-scans the giv reg created in pass 1 as a biv.
  If its "initial value" is a plain REG (copy of a pseudo: `giv = q` canonicalized by cse2 to `giv = P`) the lreg dump shows length x2;
  if the initial value is "complex" (`(plus reg const)` left by cse2, e.g. when the store is written as `q[cpv-1]` with q = apv+1)
  it is not doubled.  `-fno-rerun-loop-opt` / `-fno-rerun-cse-after-loop` remove the doubling (debug only).
  Draft `q = apv + 1; { q[cpv-1] = pCur; cpv++; }` (store first, complex init): allocation right, aligned 5, but the hoisted &apv pseudo is
  spilled (sw v1,0x320(sp)) because q = sp+0x204 is not a provider for sp+0x200.  Need BOTH a provider (pseudo == sp+0x200 live early, 1 extra use) AND a complex init.
* `do { q[cpv] = pCur; } while (0); cpv++;` gives ppv 8 refs (depth-2 weight), which fixes the whole s6/s7/fp rotation (aligned 7, all registers match!)
  but leaves a `.p2align 3` nop before the join label (the fake loop's NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG aligns the next label) and swaps `addiu s6`/`addiu s5`.
  (The next-label rule: shorten_branches aligns the first CODE_LABEL after any LOOP_BEG note, even for a deleted do-while(0).)
* gcse (M-R) hoists ANY lone `sp+K` computed after a loop to the end of the first block unless some path out of the loop skips it
  (an early `return` inside the loop blocks it; mt8/u2 micro tests).
* Micro test harness idea: /tmp-style tiny functions with `int pad[128]` before the array reproduce the hoist/EQUIV/doubling in <1s.

   addresses inside the call expression (`*cursor`, `apt[i+1]`, explicit cursor, pq pointer variants ... 50+ forms) always gave 34-59.
   Loop bound `i < sizeof apt / sizeof *apt` (unsigned sltiu) while `i < 4` stays signed.  (aligned 34 -> 4)
2. The prologue (M4 init): rows 1,2 of m4.m are stored through $s0 (= &m4 pseudo) but row 0, the three w-zero stores are $sp-relative and
   pos.w is $s0-relative.  Diagnosis: integrate.c substitutes the constant `this` into an inlined insn only if the insn is still
   recognizable afterwards, cse1 never folds `(plus reg K)` MEM addresses, so which stores are reg-based depends on how each
   was written.  Reproduced by `M4 *pm = &m4;` (a real pointer variable) with: `func_00256D18(&m4.pos.q, q1); pm->m = m;`
   `m4.m.r[0].i[3] = m4.m.r[1].i[3] = m4.m.r[2].i[3] = 0;` (by name!) `pm->pos.f[3] = 1.0f;`.  The `register ... asm("$17")` pin on q1 is
   not needed.  HIT keeps `int unk54[5]` tail so that sizeof==0x70 (frame 0x2F0).
General tricks:
* A diagnostic that finds this kind of mismatch: cast the suspicious stores to `volatile` once (`((volatile U*)&m.r[1])->q = ...`).
  If that matches, the target has stores that are NOT folded to $sp-relative addresses; then look for a natural source that keeps a
  pointer variable in the address (`M4 *pm = &m4; pm->...`) for exactly those stores and the by-name form for the others.
* The .rtl dump of the caller shows which stores kept the `this` pseudo (reg/v) vs the folded `(plus (reg 112) K)` form.

## func_001CA118 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001CA118.cpp)
Was aligned 11 (old draft had a `$20` pin on the key plus a `sw` instruction asm). Final source: plain C++, one pin
(`register IDNODE *leaf asm("$22")`), one empty asm barrier with an allocation-only input (`"r"(leaf)`) at the end.

What it took (each was a separate hurdle; all reusable):
1. Loop 2 (clear old owner marks) is NOT rotated in the target (`top: id test; owner test; sw; b top` with the parent
   load in the `b` delay slot) and its `-1` is hoisted into a register, yet it has NO `.p2align 3` nop in front of the head.
   `for`/`while(1)` give the rotated shape (they get a NOTE_INSN_LOOP_VTOP, jump.c duplicate_loop_exit_test rotates them);
   `do { if(..)break; ..} while(1)` is un-rotated but gets `.p2align 3` (a nop here) and, worse, breaks loop 1 (see 2).
   A `goto again;` loop with an explicit `int none = -1;` declared in front of it gives exactly the target: no loop note
   -> no alignment nop, and the named constant is the register that holds -1 (cse keeps it because `beq` can't take an imm).
2. gcse/PRE interplay: loop 1 (`for (; pn && pn->id != -1; pn = pn->parent) a[--c] = pn->id;`) only gets its PRE'd
   `daddu a0,v0,zero` copy (id reused from the loop-test load) if NO later loop that shares the same pseudo (`pn`) has
   `mem(pn)` as its first (anticipatable) load. Any un-rotated loop 2 that starts with `pn->id` kills loop 1's PRE
   ("PRE: redundant insn 63" disappears from the .gcse dump). Cure: write loop 1 by hand with the temporary
   (`v = pn->id; ... t = pn->id; if (t == -1) break; v = t;`), which reproduces the PRE shape without PRE.
3. Loop 3 tail: `if (pn->owner) break; pn->owner = p;` made reorg turn `bnez; sw; b top` into `beql v0,zero,top` with the sw
   in the likely slot. `if (pn->owner == 0) pn->owner = p; else break;` keeps `bnez exit; nop; b top (sw in slot)` as in the target.
4. Register order of the $s regs = global.c priority: floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length. Natural order was
   [r, 1, 0x6C, key, -1, &D]; target is [1, 0x6C, key, -1, r, &D]. r needs len 37-38 but has 32 -> pinned r to $22.
   Side effect of a pin: n (call result in the notify block) then takes the pinned, "ever live", free $s6 instead of
   $s1 (global.c prefers already-live regs). An empty asm with `"r"(leaf)` at the end of the function keeps leaf live
   so n can't share it. (Without the pin and with only that asm, leaf gets $s2: priority too high.)
   Use `lens.sh`-style inspection: `.lreg` dump prints "Register N used R times across L insns" = the allocator's R and L.
- Struct meaning: LO+0xC vptr, LO+0x3C = current node in an id tree (IDNODE {id,parent,pad,owner@0x10}, id -1 = root).
  D_003DD490+0x3F08 = row table (0x6C-byte rows, index from func_001C9950(id)), +0x3F0C = root node.
  Virtual 28 returns a child array (count in v0), virtual 25 on each child takes (key, 1). Empty class ARENA with
  ctor func_001AE478 / dtor func_001AE4A0(this, 2).

## func_00199D48 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_00199D48.c)
Was aligned 5 with two VU pins (vf3 back, vf5 direction). Final: one pin (vf5 on the direction inside Project), plain C.
- The old residual (selected-endpoint pointer in $a0 instead of $v0, `lwc1`/`sll` order, `lui v0`/`lw v1,0(v0)` vs target
  `lui v1`/`lw v1,%lo(v1)`) all came from ONE thing: the target's `lw v1,%lo(v1)` shares the register of its dying %hi `lui`.
  Fix = asm input constraint `"rV"(D_002BF8A8)` on the `qmtc2.ni` (notes/120000.md laneB22, func_0012D220): 'V' counts as memory at
  expand (MEM is not forced into a pseudo) but an offsettable MEM fails 'V' at reload, so reload loads it into a reg at the asm
  through the dying %hi register. With the load no longer a separate local-alloc pseudo, lui1/lui2/lw all land in $v1 and the
  selected pointer gets $v0; sched2 then puts lwc1 before sll (WAR on $v1).
- Callee-side evidence that local-alloc never lets an output reuse an input that dies in the same insn (the same-insn
  death/birth counts as overlap): `lw $3,0($2)` style loads are the norm; only reload/"rV" produces the shared form.
- Dropping the vf3 pin (back) was free once the load was fixed. The vf5 pin is still needed: unpinned, local-alloc gives
  direction vf4 / lift vf5 (swapped); operand permutations of the Project asm and statement orders don't change it.
- Local-alloc model (verified on this block): quantities are taken in order of floor_log2(refs)*refs/(death-birth) (ticks of
  2 per insn in sched1 order), each gets the lowest free hard reg; a quantity born in the insn where another dies conflicts with it.

## func_001C6DE8 - near-miss (build/hand_wip/func_001C6DE8.lane9.v1.cpp, aligned 17, policy-clean: no pins, only VU asm)
Old drafts used pins + `lq` asm. Clean version found these (all reusable):
- Arena object as `struct ARENA { char pad[2]; }` (BLKmode => its stack slot is allocated at DECLARATION, in front of the
  union `scalar`; the empty struct is a QImode scalar and its slot is allocated late, when &arena is first taken, giving
  the wrong 0x60/0x70 order). Frame becomes 0x110 as in the target. count/objects (address-taken params) get late slots 0x80/0x84,
  and p/upper/lower are spill slots 0x90/0xA0/0xB0.
- One `asm volatile` per VU add (vadda+vmaddx fused), source order lower (lo) then upper (hi): volatile keeps sched1 from swapping
  them; plus `scalar.q = h; ... scalar.q = l;` union stores for the two 0x70 sqc2.
- The three successes must be three separate `*out = X; dtor; return 1;` blocks and the failures separate `{dtor; return 0;}`
  blocks (no shared `result` variable / no goto): jump2 cross-jumping then merges the tails exactly like the target
  (fail block first, then `result=point` block, shared `sq v0,0(s0)` in the dtor call's delay slot). A shared `result` pseudo
  gets a copy-preference for $a0 (from the param pseudo) and lands in $a0 instead of $v0.
- Remaining diff: s0/s1 swap (out should be s0, &arena s1). Cause: the PRE copy `r170 = &arena` is inserted at the END of bb0
  (after the ctor call) so the ctor-arg pseudo crosses a call and takes s0 in local-alloc; the target copies a0->s1 BEFORE the
  call (like func_001CA118's `daddu s2,a0,zero`, where sched1 moved the PRE copy above the call). Also VU prologue order
  (lqc2 vf1 before qmtc2 vf3; scalar sqc2 before the lower sqc2).
- UPDATE func_001C6DE8: build/hand_wip/func_001C6DE8.lane9.v2.cpp = aligned 13 (24 positional). Dropping `volatile` from the two VU asms
  fixed the whole prologue (the PRE copy of &arena can then be hoisted above the ctor call by sched1: a volatile asm is a full
  scheduling barrier and the PRE copy is inserted at the END of bb0, i.e. after the asms, so it got stuck after the call and
  ctor-arg pseudo crossed the call -> s0/s1 swap). Without volatile sched1 picks the asm with the smaller reg-weight
  (INSN_REG_WEIGHT: +1 per reg born, -1 per REG_DEAD) first, ties -> lowest luid. To get lower-before-upper write the asms in the order
  [upper, scalar.q=h, lower, scalar.q=l]: the *later* source asm is the last user of `axis`, so it has weight 0 vs upper's +1.
  (Adjacent `scalar.q=h; scalar.q=l;` gets the first store deleted by cse - keep an asm between them.)
  Remaining: h/l land in vf3/vf2 instead of vf2/vf3 (local-alloc priority: l's range is shorter), and `sw a1,0x80`/`sw a2,0x84` entry
  stores come in the opposite order (target: a2 before ra-save, a1 in the jal delay slot).

  target's `daddu a0,s0; daddu a1,s0; addiu a0,a0,16` argument setup (the index*8+base operand order and the
  unfolded `+16` both come from this). Calling memmove directly always folds to `addiu a0,s0,16` / `(j*8+16)+ae`.
- Last 2 diffs were delay-slot/schedule order of the three stores to ae[j+1]: write `.k = 0` BEFORE `.bit = bit`.
- No pins, no asm. (The old draft's ByteLength asm is not needed.)
- General lesson: when the target shows `a0 = X; a1 = X; a0 += K` (or `a1 = base; a0 = a1; a0 += K`) around a call,
  wrap the call in a static inline forwarding helper taking (void*, void*, n) and pass `&arr[i+1], &arr[i]` to it.
  Probably also fixes func_001E5280 (which currently uses instruction asm) and func_001D1518-style callers.

## FUN_0018b988 (0x1AABC0) - MATCHED (src/hand/FUN_0018b988.c)
- Started from build/hand_wip/FUN_0018b988.codex18v2.c (aligned 1, only the last block differed: target is
  `addiu v0,31; jr ra; nop`, ours `jr ra; addiu v0,31`). The `register int result asm("$2")` pin there is NOT needed.
- Cause (found by reading gcc 2.95.3 reorg.c, fetched from the gcc mirror): the last `jr ra` keeps an EMPTY delay slot
  when a CODE_LABEL sits between the `li v0,31` and the return while dbr's fill_simple_delay_slots runs
  (stop_search_p stops at labels). That label is the function's return label, kept alive by *conditional* branches to it
  (jump2 can only turn unconditional `jump return_label` into `return` copies). Later relax_delay_slots/make_return_insns
  redirect those branches to the first unfilled return (here the one inside kind 1), the label dies, and the unfilled
  `jr ra; nop` stays behind with no visible label.
- Fix: write the end of the function as `result = 31; done: return result;` and let kinds 16/17 do
  `result = 0; if (err == 0) goto done;` (conditional branch to the shared return label). Kind 1 is plain
  `result = 9; return result;`. (The old draft had a separate `returnSelected:` label inside kind 1.)
- General rule: if the target ends `li v0,K; jr ra; nop` (unfilled slot, no branch target there) while ours has the slot
  filled, give some earlier path a *conditional* branch to a shared `done: return result;` at the end of the function.
- Semantics (kind, err -> message id): common codes -2->12, -4->16/21/22/24, 0->0; `Fallback(err) = (err < -9) ? 11 : 31`
  ; kinds 16/17: 0->0, negative->4/5, positive->31; kind 20: -403->35; kind 21: -100..-1->31, < -100 ->30.

## func_001BDE80 (polygon merge / hull) - near miss, build/hand_wip/func_001BDE80.lane10.v1.cpp (aligned 12, was 36; 0 pins)
Fixes found (all general):
- First loop: write it as `for (int i0 = 0; i0 < n; i0++) c += ap[i0]->c - 1;`. The old `if (n>0) {do{..}while(--remaining);}` form made
  gcse PRE/HOIST pull `lui %hi(D_002A2DD0)` to the function entry (held in $s6 across all calls, shifting every callee-saved
  register). With the `for` form nothing is hoisted (tiny repro: `if(n>0){do..while}` then call then `G` read hoists the lui,
  the `for` form does not).
- Inner write loop: loop.c would not strength-reduce `apos + k` (byte offset k, mult 1: giv benefit 4 - add_cost 4 = 0 -> "not worth
  while"). An explicit pointer reproduces the target's pointer+offset pair: `qword *pw = (qword*)((unsigned)k + (unsigned)apos);
  do { k += 16; *++pw = MulPt(...); j++; } while (j < px->c - 1);` inside `j = 0; if (j < px->c - 1) {...}` (the unsigned cast gives the
  target's `addu a0,s2,s4` operand order; the guarded do-while puts the pw init after the guard branch like the target).
  The real source probably had an index `idx` whose `idx*16` giv loop.c reduced (target s2 starts at -16 and its init is emitted
  after the polygon-loop guard, i.e. in the loop preheader) but I could not make the shift giv "worth while" (needs benefit > 4).
- MulPt: use a plain `"=j"(out)` output plus five `"j"` inputs (no pins, no `"+j"` in/out, no earlyclobber): that alone gives the
  target's vf3/vf5/vf4/vf2/vf1 assignment (the earlier draft had 5 vf pins).
- Last loop: `int p = pNew->c; while (--p >= 0) {...}` gives the target's `lw a0,0xE8; addiu a0,a0,-1` shape.
- Virtual call at the end of the sum section: `void (*pfn)(X*,qword) = (void(*)(X*,qword))pNew->vt[29].pfn;`
  `pfn((X*)((char*)pNew + pNew->vt[29].d), (u.q = k2, sum));` with `"+j"(sum), "+j"(k2)` in the vdiv/vmulq asm (allocation-only
  dependence, makes the dead VU_FLOAT home store `sqc2 vf1,0x10($sp)` come after the vmulq and land in the call delay slot).
  The fn-pointer load must precede the store to the stack slot (memory dependence), hence the separate `pfn` local.
Remaining diff (12): polygon index and `px` swap s0/s1. Cause: sched1 hoists `polygon = 0` (no dependences) into the free ALU slot at
t=1 before the first call, so its live range is long (46 insns) and its allocation priority falls below px's. In the old
`k = -16; for(polygon=0..)` form k took that slot and polygon stayed short (allocation right), but then `li s2,-16` sits before the guard.
An asm with a data dependence on apos fixes the allocation (diagnostic only) but blocks dbr from moving the init into the first jal slot.

## func_001BDE80 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001BDE80.cpp) [supersedes the near-miss section above]
Final recipe = the fixes listed above plus: (1) the polygon-loop `px` is a function-scope `register X *px asm("$17")` (the single
pin) used by both the polygon loop and the last loop, and GetMatrix() is written out by hand (an inline helper's parameter copy
creates an extra `daddu s0,s1,zero` once px is pinned); (2) what fixed the s0/s1 swap: global alloc orders pseudos by
floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length. sched1 hoists `polygon = 0` (no dependences) into the free ALU slot before the first call, so its
live range is ~46 insns and px (14 refs / 38 insns) wins s0. Sharing the counter with the first loop (ip, 16 refs/31 insns)
fixed the allocation (aligned 3, only the first loop's counter reg differed) but that is not how the target is built, so a
pin on px was used instead.
General lessons: (a) a single `register T *p asm("$17")` on the variable that wins the allocation order is much cheaper than
pinning several; pin the one that `lreg/greg` order says is wrong and declare it once at function scope. (b) pinning a LOOP COUNTER
(polygon) is bad: hard-reg user vars are not bivs, loop.c loses the `i+1` hoist. (c) -da `.greg` first line "N regs to allocate: ..."
lists pseudos in priority order; compare with `.lreg` "used X times across Y insns" to see why two pseudos swap.

## func_001DDB08 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001DDB08.cpp, natural C++, no pins, no asm)
Wave-1 near miss was aligned 2 with one register pin. The match is the sibling "SetPv with an unused dummy third param" form
(func_0013D328 / func_00143AF0): `static inline void SetPv(void *pv, const CRef &r, void *) { CRef arg(r); const CRef &ret = func_00258200(pv, arg, &D_003D4A60); }`
called as `SetPv(D_003E0FAC, ref, &D_003D4A60)` inside a `{ CRef ref(0x16,0); ... }` block, and the tail calls `func_00165E38(&D_003D4A60)` with the
address spelled out again (no `G *pg` local at all). The unused param gets its pseudo at inline entry, so the hi/lo of the global is built once as a
pseudo that global-alloc puts in s0 (pv ends up s1). Variants with a `G *pg = &D_003D4A60` local, a used pg param, or an explicit `ref.~CRef()` were aligned 9-11.
General: when a CRef/Send-style function with a game-state global keeps %hi/%lo in s0, try the dummy-param SetPv and spell the global's address out at each use.

## func_00194F70 - near miss (build/hand_wip/func_00194F70.w2lane1.v1.cpp, aligned 12 (was 40), no pins, no asm; one empty `do{}while(0)` structure hack)
Root cause of the old s1/s2 swap (target: i/fWrap/hi(D_002DEB30) in s1, a in s2; ours had them reversed) was the global-alloc priority of `a`
(5*44/121 = 1.82 vs i = 3*12/30 = 1.2), and REG_N_REFS is weighted by loop depth + 1: every `a` ref inside the inner do-while was counted at depth 2
(x3). Target must have the whole inner search/found path at depth 1: write the inner search as a goto loop
(`goto test; top: ... test: if (--j >= 0) goto top;`) so FOUND is at loop depth 1 (a: 44 -> 32 weighted refs, i: 1.2 stays). That alone flips the allocation
(i=s1, a=s2, hi=s1) but loses the inner loop's NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG, i.e. the `.p2align 3` nop at the loop head that the target has.
Getting the alignment back without changing depth: `do { top: x0 = e->x0; a = Entry(ae, j); } while (0);` around only the first two statements. A do{}while(0)
keeps a LOOP_BEG note (final.c emits `.p2align 3` there; it also acts as a sched barrier, hence the x0 load inside it to keep `lw e->x0` before the `addu a`).
The label `top:` must be INSIDE the do{} so the .p2align lands before it. The note also adds +1 weight to the refs inside it (ae and the 0x30 constant: fixes the s6/s7 swap).
Other fixes that were needed (each one dropped the count): single store `a->x18 = ex + d` reached through `goto store` into the else-if arm (one store site: a loses
a weighted ref and codegen shares the store tail like the target); a local `float ex = e->x18`/`av = a->x18` read once and used for both d and the store (kills a
gcse copy `mov.s f3,f2`); `i = ExistingCount(p); for (; --i >= 0;)` (pre-decrement; in-place `addiu s1,s1,-1`).
Remaining 12: (1) prologue `addiu v1,s4,0x4680` (target) vs `v0` (ours) for the work.owned temp, (2) sched2 puts `addiu fp,s1,-1` before the e-chain in ours (target:
after it, in the `b` delay slot), (3) loop-2 tail: target has the new-link result copied `daddu a2,v0,zero` (r lives in a2, not v0), a2=`a` set before the jal and a0 in the delay slot
(ours: a0 first, a2 in the slot, r in v0). Tried for (3) without effect: r as long/int, `if (!(r = f())) break`, r declared at function scope, store-then-test, loop-2 `b` pointer (loses the s2 copy).
Sched1 explanation for the a0/a2 order: ties on weight go to lower luid; target needs the a2 move earlier (maybe `a` dies at the arg move in sched1, i.e. the later store goes through another pseudo).
Also tried for the remaining 12 (all no change): loop-2 variants (`ae[j]` everywhere, block-scoped `b`, prov local, `r` as long/int/void*, one function-scope `t` shared with the
1619A0 object arg to give r a $6 preference), outer loop forms (do-while, while, `--i` in the header), `j = n` / `e =` order, work.owned placement.
Useful facts for whoever continues: sched1 ties go to the lower luid; weight (REG_DEAD of the source) prefers moves whose source pseudo dies, so the target's
`daddu a2,s2; jal; daddu a0,s4` order suggests the pseudo passed as arg 3 dies at the call (the later `sw a2,0x10(s2)` goes through another pseudo that shares s2).
In sched2 block 5 (loop head) ours issues `fp = i-1` in the mult stall (cycle 2); the target issues it after the e-chain, so there it was not ready/present in that block.

## func_00195368  (near-miss; build/hand_wip/func_00195368.w2lane2.v1.c, aligned 27, no pins, VU asm helpers only)
Big finding: the target's `mtlo entries; ... madd s6,i,0x30` is gcc's `*mul_acc_si_r5900` (combine) and needs ALL of:
 1. count / entries / i are plain locals (params + loop var) that get SPILLED (slots 0x1F0/0x1F4/0x1F8 in regno order; the `sw a2/a3,0x1F0/4(sp)` at entry are
    just param spills; NO struct needed - the old drafts' `struct INPUT` was wrong).  Reload picks the "accumulate in LO" alternative for a spilled operand => `lw v1,0x1F4; mtlo v1; lw v1,0x1F8; madd`.
 2. combine only forms mul_acc when the addend register is NOT defined by an insn in the same basic block as the mult (LOG_LINKS): a pseudo set before the loop is fine, a load in the loop body is not
    (`g1`-style micro tests: `*pe + i` never combines; `e + i` with e a param/earlier pseudo does).
 3. loop.c must NOT strength-reduce `&sources[i]`.  It does when the `li 48` constant is hoisted in loop pass 2: the movable test is life*95*savings >= "N real insns" of the outer loop (-da .loop: "Loop from..: N real insns", pass 2).
    Pass-2 count must be >= 96.  The old drafts' pins/asm were irrelevant; what raised the count in my draft: a float->int bit move via a union (`Bits()` helper feeding qmtc2) and splitting
    the `vadda`/`vmaddx` pair into two asm statements (the original has one intrinsic per VU op; Ghidra shows `_vadda` and `_vmaddbc` separately).  (Test: 3 empty asm("") also flips it.)
 4. `(int)amount` / `(float)n` in plain C already gives `cvt.w.s f1,f20; mfc1; mtc1; nop; cvt.s.w` exactly (no CurrentRound asm).  Statement order: `amount = rate * dt` BEFORE the func_0011D448(0,1) call (mul.s sits in the jal delay slot).
 5. offset compare is `spec->offset.lo != spec->offset.hi` (both via s1); declare `ageRange` before `offsetRange` so the 5-ref/59-vs-60-len tie spills ageRange (0x1FC) and gives offsetRange fp.
 6. `array[produced]` indexing instead of the explicit running pointers made loop.c merge ages/ends into one giv (worse, 66); keep three pointer locals.
Remaining diffs (aligned 27): (a) produced is s3, ageOut s4 (target s4 / s3): priorities produced .6095 (16 refs, len 105) > ageOut .569 (11, 58) > endOut .541; target needs ageOut > produced > endOut,
 i.e. produced len 113-118, or ageOut len <= 54.  (b) VU numbering: target qmtc2 -> vf2, lqc2 -> vf1, vmulx vf1,vf1,vf2x; ours swapped; target vmaddx result in vf3, ours vf1; also lqc2/sqc2 order in the `if` body.
 Tried for (a): declaring ageOut last (no change).

## func_001E9C88  (no progress beyond lane 8; build/hand_wip/func_001E9C88.w2lane2.v1.c = `do { q[cpv] = pCur; } while (0); cpv++;` form, aligned 7)
Re-derived why lane 8's recipes stall (all with q = apv at the top so P = sp+0x200 gets REG_EQUAL):
 * gcse hoists P (even a lone final-call sp+0x200) to the end of bb0; a provider pseudo earlier in bb0 turns it into `P = q` + REG_EQUAL => reload rematerialises, no spill (frame 0x3F0).  A *user* variable
   `ppv = apv` at the top (bb0) also works as provider but then its init is in bb0 (target: after the guard bc1f).
 * store-first => giv init is a plain REG copy => second loop pass doubles ppv's live length (59 -> 118) => priority .119 < cseg .193 => s6/s7/fp rotation.
 * `do { q[cpv] = pCur; } while (0); cpv++;` gives refs 8 (depth 3) => allocation right (all other registers match), remaining 7 diffs: `.p2align 3` nop before the join label (first label after the
   fake loop's LOOP_BEG note) and loop.c emits `ppv += 4` BEFORE `cpv += 1` (LOOP_END note between them => sched1 cannot swap them, target: cpv then ppv).  Putting `cpv++` inside the fake loop gives
   cpv 18 refs (.3077) > pseg (.2778) and swaps s4/s5.  A user-declared ppv pointer (bb0) gets s4 and no spill but wrong order.
 * -fno-gcse makes everything but the &pos2 hoist come out right (ppv gets s6), so the original really only differs in how P/ppv are formed.
Dead ends this round: q for search/store/final in all 8 combinations, `(void**)aseg)[128+cpv]` index forms (P still hoisted+spilled), explicit do-while guard (same as for), user ppv in the guard block (frame 0x3E0, lui not spilled).

## Tooling notes (Windows)
 * matchtool accepts `// SLY2_CFLAGS: ...` in the first 2000 bytes (e.g. `-fno-gcse`) - handy for diagnosis only.
 * Check takes 10-20 s when other agents are running; python subprocess calls to `bash` hit a different bash, so drive scripts via mt.py (direct python exe) and shell scripts for dumps.
 * `build/lane_scratch/w2lane2/prio2.py FILE` prints the global-alloc order with refs/len and the hard reg (or SPILL); `loopcnt.sh` prints loop.c "real insns" per pass.

## func_001C6FD8 / func_001D2610: only re-checked the existing drafts (aligned 101 / 114); diffs are dominated by global schedule/alloc order in the VU-heavy bodies, not started.

## General lessons (this lane)
 1. mtlo/madd (`*mul_acc_si_r5900`): spilled-pseudo addend + addend defined outside the bb + loop.c not reducing the giv (hold the `li` const in the loop with >= 96 real insns in loop pass 2).
 2. Allocation-only `"r"(ptr)` operands on an existing asm statement in an inner loop add +3 refs (loop depth weight) to that pointer's global-alloc priority: a cheap way to reorder s-regs without pins; duplicated `"j"(v)` inputs reorder vf numbering.
 3. A register pin on a variable that loop.c turns into a biv adds a temp copy and shifts other allocations (worse than no pin).
 4. `.p2align 3` after a fake `do{}while(0)` store comes from shorten_branches aligning the first CODE_LABEL after the LOOP_BEG note; and a LOOP_END note between a store and `cpv++` stops sched1 reordering `ppv+=4`/`cpv+=1`.

## func_001999D0 -> near miss aligned 8 (8/221 positional), build/hand_wip/func_001999D0.w2lane3.v3.cpp
One pin (`register qword res asm("vf1")`), otherwise natural C plus commented allocation-only tricks. Fresh rewrite (struct layout from codex18fresh1) using the matched
func_0011B830 idioms (Len = dot asm + separate sqrt asm, Div, `F()` = mtc1 asm taking the qword, fallback `D_002A2DA0` loaded BEFORE the compare). Path 181 -> 63 -> 24 -> 13 -> 8:
- One shared normalize result pinned to vf1 (`res`) gives the target's shape: fallback `lq v0` in the bc1 delay slot + `qmtc2.ni v0,vf1`, shared (cross-jumped) between kind 1 and kind 2. Without the pin: 28.
- Kind 1's running sum in vf3 saved/restored around BOTH calls (sqc2/lqc2 0x120) is caller-save, not an explicit stack variable. gcc only does that when
  CALLER_SAVE_PROFITABLE: `4*calls_crossed < REG_N_REFS` (refs weighted by loop depth+1). The plain local has 7 vs 8 needed. A duplicated `"j"(v)` operand on the dot asm
  (Dot(a,b) with a==b, plus one more on Div) raises it. 63 -> 40.
- Kind 2 reloads the summed vector from memory a second time for the divide (`lqc2 vf2,0x110(sp)` in the bc1f delay slot): cse merges the loads;
  `__asm__("" : "+m"(w.sum.q))` between Len and Div (empty, non-volatile, commented) splits them; the second load must be a named local (`qword v2=w.sum.q;`)
  defined BEFORE the branch, so it is a multi-block pseudo allocated after `l` (gives v2=vf2, l!=vf2).
- Local-alloc/global-alloc facts used: priority = floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/life with size 2 for TImode; a pseudo that dies twice or crosses a block goes to global-alloc (after all
  block-local ones); an asm clobber (`"vf2"` on the second loop's Add) is a way to push a short-lived pseudo to the next register (loop-2 sum vf2 -> vf3, as in the target).
- Remaining 8 diffs: (a) kind-2 Len input should be vf4 (ours vf1) and its sqrt result vf1 (ours vf3): the codex draft pinned them; Len2 with earlyclobber/clobber/extra
  sqrt operands, an input loaded before `if(!found)` (17), clobber-carrying barrier all moved things around but never reached v=vf4,d=vf1,one=vf2,l=vf1;
  (b) the zero-vector address for kind 2: target `lui v0;addiu v0;lq v0,0(v0)`, ours `lui v1;addiu v1;lq v0,0(v1)` (TI data pseudo has 4x the priority of the address pseudo, so it takes v0 first; a global/multi-block D would share). 
  Variants tried without effect: address in a named pointer, named `zero` local, struct assignment `w.sum=D_002A2DD0`.

## func_001DF0A0 -> near miss aligned 11 (build/hand_wip/func_001DF0A0.w2lane3.v1.c; lane4 v1 with aligned 8 stays the best number)
New structure gets the target's VU ORDER (B1 A2 S1 B2 Q1 S2, exact load order) which lane4's fused Len could not (A2 landed after S1). Remaining: out0/pos one register too high (vf5/vf6), the
v0/v1 swap (&e->head vs qmfc2 scratch) and the prologue `daddu s5,a1` / `sq s4` order.
- VU pseudos are allocated by local-alloc only if they die once; a shared pseudo temp that dies twice goes to global-alloc LAST (vf6/vf7).
- Scheduler rank (sched1 and sched2): priority, [sched1: reg weight], number of dependents, LUID (= sched1 order for sched2). Anti/output dependences cost 1 here.
  A hard `"vf3"` clobber creates NO dependence between two dot asms, so with per-call temps A2 (4 dependents) always beats B1 (3). A SHARED pseudo temp `one` written by both dot asms
  (operand unused in the template, template hard-codes $vf3, plus the `"vf3"` clobber that keeps the other pseudos off vf3) gives B1->B2 an output dependence: priorities and dependents tie and LUID puts B1 first = target order.
- The target is a perfect 2-issue packing (lqc2|addiu, lqc2|daddu a0, A1|lqc2, B1|A2, S1|B2, Q1|S2, lw a1|N, then the arg moves), so ANY extra dependence between the VU asms
  (memory clobbers, volatile, dummy dependency operands) serializes the chain and lets the arg moves intrude (20-29). Do not use them.
- dir (A2 result) gets vf2 only with a high allocation priority: a duplicated `"j"(dir)` operand on the second dot asm.
- Pinning `&e->head` to $3 (experiment) removes the v0/v1 swap: reload picks the qmfc2 scratch = lowest free GPR, so H must already sit in $3 (natural cause still unknown).
- Prologue order `daddu s5,a1` before `sq s4`: sched2 tie (both prio 5); `sq s4` has 3 dependents (the `i=0` insn), `daddu s5` 2; target needs the opposite. func_001F5990 declared int/unprototyped,
  for-loop forms, parameter copies: no change.
- Dead ends: TI-GPR asm qmfc2 for bits (`qmfc2.ni t2 ; por v1,zero,t2`), memory-clobber chains, A2 dependency operand on the dot result, declaration order permutations.

  * func_0011B830 etc. declared to return M3 by value (hidden return pointer), NOT `M3*` -> removes the v0/v1 swap in the 48-byte copy.
