    (stores `t.rows[i]=vi; mat.rows[i]=vi;` interleaved per row) used as `current->matrix = frame*rot;`.
    The dead copy `t` is the 0xE0 slot, `mat` the 0xB0 slot (temp slot freed by the previous call temp).  This interleaving decided
    the sched1 LUID order of the stores and thereby the vf numbering of the b0/b1/b2/z pseudos (local-alloc priority = refs*log2/life).
  * `CHANNEL *spin=p->orientation; spin->velocity=...` as a separate variable (not reusing `current`) fixed a0/a1 numbering.

### func_001C5148: MATCHED -> src/hand/func_001C5148.cpp (no pins; VU asm + `"rV"` constraint on the dt word only)
- Last two fixes after the 6/265 state: (1) `VU_FLOAT(int bits)` asm input constraint `"rV"` instead of `"r"` (keeps the dt load as a memory
  operand of the asm so reload emits the `lw` into a reload register next to the qmtc2; this decided v0/v1 for mfc1/base/lw and the
  lqc2-vs-qmtc2 order in the first VU block); (2) `spin` variable for the second p->orientation load.
- General lessons:
  * Ready-list tie-break chain in sched1/sched2 (from haifa-sched.c rank_for_schedule): priority, [reg weight pre-reload], class vs last
    scheduled insn (independent/latency-1 beats data-dependent), number of dependents, then LUID. Equal-priority leaf stores therefore come out in
    program order, and the store order decides local-alloc life lengths (priority = floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/(death-birth)), i.e. vf numbering.
  * A dead local copy `t` stored right after each row in an inline matrix product (func_00122F00 style) reproduces a second 48-byte stack slot
    AND the store interleaving; write it as t first, then the NRV `mat`.
  * Declaring a callee that returns a struct as `M3 f(...)` (hidden pointer) instead of `M3 *f(M3 *, ...)` removed a v0/v1 swap in a 48-byte
    copy (the pointer return conflicted with $v0 in local-alloc).
  * Locals in the innermost block (not a struct WORK at function scope) make the VU_FLOAT temp slot sp+0 the first slot.

## func_001DF5B0: MATCHED -> src/hand/func_001DF5B0.cpp (no pins; VU asm, one volatile empty asm with operands, allocation-neutral)
Started from lane3.v1 (aligned 27, WORK/PHASE-union + MEASUREMENT struct). Rewritten natural; the fixes, in order of effect:
- `dir` = plain `qword` local (not a memory struct): it crosses func_0011B830, gets spilled (spill slot = the 0x120 slot; `float dist` is
  address-taken and lazily allocated AFTER all nested-block slots, hence 0x110); target's `sqc2`+`lq a1,0x120` came out right.
- Locals in nested blocks and callees returning M3/M4 by value: the stack pool 0x60..0x110 (facing M3, render M4, tmp M4; then matrix M4 and tmp M4
  at 0x60/0xA0) is just gcc's temp-slot reuse+split+merge (free adjacent temp slots are merged and big free slots split), no union needed.
- `M4 *pm=&matrix` declared in the else branch only (rows 1,2 and the corner w via pm) gives the target's mixed sp/s2 addressing.
- The VU_FLOAT(radius) lw-asm made `volatile` (barrier in sched1): all earlier loads stay before the call args, so the camera address pseudo dies at the
  `a0=cam` copy and local-alloc puts it straight into $a0 (and the bound pointer in $v1); also moved `lui` first in the fall-through so dbr fills the
  beqz delay slot with it. Declare `CAMERA *cam=&D_002DDF40` and `boundPosition` BEFORE the radius temp.
- `extern qword D_002A2DC0;` must not be `const`: a const (unchanging) load had no dependence on the spill store, so sched2 hoisted `lq a2` above it.
- Length's asm lists its input twice ("j"(v),"j"(v)) as in func_00151C38/Dot helpers: changes the reload register of the spilled operand (vf4 vs vf3).

## func_001E6F60: MATCHED -> src/hand/func_001E6F60.cpp (VU asm helpers + ONE commented allocation-only empty asm on `time`)
Path-closest-point search (mode 0: segments, mode 1: vertices). Progress 52 -> 35 -> 25 -> 16 -> MATCH; what each step was:
- Stack home of the three long-lived TI pseudos (start 0x60, end 0x70, point 0x80) follows the order in which they are first address-wrapped
  (`(mem (addressof (reg N)))` in the -dr dump; the final regno is assigned in that order), NOT declaration order. In the loop head
  `Sub(end=TransformPoint(..), start)` followed by `Sub(point,start)` gives start<end<point; `end=T(); Sub(end,start)` gives end<start<point.
  (statement-order / Delta(from,to) swaps of the operands only permute the three slots; find the permutation you need with a small search.)
- Where the Dot/Length VU result is converted to a float matters for LOCAL-alloc: `mtc1 %1,%0 : "=f"(f) : "r"(v)` with v a TI qword makes the Dot
  result a TI pseudo that local-alloc puts in a GPR pair ($v0/$v1) and reload then picks the VU register (lowest free that is not occupied by the
  globally allocated `direction`) -> direction ends in vf2 and the matrix rows reload in the wrong order. Splitting it in two asm statements,
  `qmfc2.ni %0,%1 : "=r"(int) : "j"(v)` then `mtc1`, keeps the Dot result a VU-class qty: local-alloc gives it vf2 and global-alloc has to give the
  direction vf3 (as in the target) and even the sched2 order of the four matrix-row reloads then comes out right. In THIS function the quotient needs
  the split form while Length() needs the single-asm "r" form (they are different helpers in the original, apparently).
- Normalize with the output tied to the length operand (`"+j"(length)` returned) puts vmulq's destination in the length register.
- Dot's `one` output must not be early-clobber here (target's second dot reuses vf3 for `one` while vf3 is also both inputs).
- FP callee-saved order (time f22 / best f23) is global-alloc priority floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length: best 5 refs/78 insns outranks time 5/150.
  The target has time higher, i.e. time needs >=8 weighted refs. `time=u*a; time+=t*b;` flips it but changes the code; an empty
  `__asm__("" : "+f"(time))` at the top of the loop body (one use + one set in the loop, weight 2 each) flips it with no code change. Used once, commented.
- Search tooling that found most of this: build/lane_scratch/w2lane4/search.py (parallel text-substitution search over spec json) + `-da` dumps (dump.sh).

## func_001E6A30: MATCHED -> src/hand/func_001E6A30.cpp (VU asm only, no pins, no allocation-only asm)
Natural rewrite (every earlier draft used `register ... asm("$f12")` pins and instruction-level asm). Path: first draft 179 diffs -> 117 -> 17 -> 3 -> MATCH.
What mattered, in order:
- `fraction` reused for the final time value (`fraction = (1-fraction)*t0 + fraction*t1`) -> lands in $f12 (call-argument preference through the
  func_001E63C0(p, fraction) copy), no pin needed. Separate `time` variable puts fraction in f3.
- `previous = index; continue;` written in BOTH branches (each branch gets its own delay-slot `daddu`), `backward = 1`/`forward = 1` after the if/else.
  A shared tail `previous = index` produces a join block with one copy.
- `const M4 *basis = &matrix;` declared INSIDE the loop body (and again inside the direction block): loop.c hoists it to the preheader, which is exactly
  where the target has the `addiu s3,sp,0x10` (delay slot of the jump into the loop) and it changes global-alloc priority basis vs space (s3/s4).
  Declared before the loop it is scheduled early by sched1 and takes s4.
- Rotate = in-place "+j" on the vector, row 0 read from `matrix.rows[0]` (direct sp) and rows 1,2 from `basis->` (pointer) -> the mixed sp/s3 addressing.
- Local-alloc priority is refs-based: listing the same VU operand twice in an asm ("j"(v),"j"(v)) doubles its reference count and made the loaded
  `segment` outrank the length in local-alloc (G->vf1, length->vf2, target is the reverse). Write `vmul.xyz %0,%2,%2` with ONE operand.
- Normalize with the output tied to the length ("+j"): the vdiv/vmulq asm works in place on the length register. Passing `segment` BY VALUE (union
  `U`) gives the unread temp copy `sqc2 vf2,0x50(sp)` (same slot as the VU_FLOAT temps of the position block).
- The two TI->float conversions must be the one-asm form `mtc1 %1,%0 : "=f"(f) : "r"(vu_qword)` (the compiler then emits `qmfc2.ni` as a reload move):
  the explicit two-asm form (qmfc2 asm + mtc1 asm, as needed by 1E6F60's quotient) made the distance bits reuse $v0 and, through the $v0 WAR edge in sched2,
  pulled `mtc1 v0,f1` and the temp store into the wrong order. With ToFloatR everywhere the distance bits take $a0 and sched2 leaves the low-priority stores last.
- Debug method that paid off: `-da -fsched-verbose-5` dumps; sched2's dependence table shows the hard-register edges that decide the order of the leftovers.

## func_001D2E18: NOT matched. Best natural draft build/hand_wip/func_001D2E18.w2lane4.v13.cpp = 44/397 instructions differ
(earlier drafts in the lane file: 232-256 diffs and all used register pins / `mult` asm). Instruction sequence is now identical except
for ordering inside two blocks and a few register numbers; nothing pinned, only VU asm helpers. What got it from ~358 to 46 (each step found in dumps):
- Vector helpers must go through the `VV` wrapper struct (conversion ctor takes `const qword &`). The extra surviving copies push the contact loop over
  loop.c's insn limit (~95 insns) -> NO strength reduction: `entry = &p->entries[i]` is recomputed with `addiu 0x60; mult; addu` each iteration like the target.
- `Evaluate(D_002C2120, t)` with a `const POLY &` parameter (reference bound inline): cse1 folds the offset-0 coefficient to a direct symbol load (own `lui`)
  while b,c go through the pointer; loop.c hoists the pointer + its HIGH. Same trick for `p->traction` / `p->limits` (`Clamp(Evaluate(p->traction, ..), p->limits)`):
  gcse PRE inserts the p+0x520 / p+0x530 pointer computations before the loop (spilled to 0xF4, held in s7) - do NOT declare them as locals before the loop.
- `rotation` pointer: use `&phase.rotation` inline at its only use (a declared local gets a low regno and lands in the first spill slot, target has it after normal/previous).
- Spill slot order = regno order; the regno of a pseudo that is bound to a `VV(const qword&)` conversion is assigned when it is first address-wrapped (reverse declaration
  order at scope end). `VV normal = ...` (not `qword`) puts normal at 0xD0 and previous at 0xE0. Caller-save slots (`force`, 0x100) come after all spill slots.
- `qword normal,previous` as pseudos (not memory `U` locals) is required: spill stores have no alias set and keep their order vs the p->space load in sched2.
- `float ratio = depth/(max-min); float fraction = Clamp(ratio,0,1)` with TWO separate hit->depth loads (do not cache depth in a local) gives hit=s1/point=s2/entry=s0
  (priority = refs*log2(refs)*size/len; a cached depth made hit outrank entry).
- `VV other = func_0022D348(...)` (plain conversion, not an asm Vector()) puts the TI->VU move into the first bc1f delay slot like the target.
- dt as raw word: `qmtc2` with an `int` bit-pun operand (`*(const int *)&clock->dt`) gives the `lw` straight into a GPR (a `float` operand gives lwc1+mfc1);
  the final read of the clock goes through a `const CLOCK &` parameter (pointer + offset 4), not through the cached clock pointer.
- InverseRotate: list the asm outputs in the order one,d0,d1,d2 (+j r last): local-alloc allocates tied priority-0 outputs in DEscending qty order, which decided vf5..vf8.
- Clamp as `if(v<lo) return lo; else if(hi<v) return hi; else return v;` matches every clamp site; the assign-style variants do not.
Remaining diffs (all scheduling/regnames): (1) rotation/limits spill slots 0xF0/0xF4 come out swapped (gcse creates the PRE pseudos in hash order), (2) post-loop block:
the target schedules the contactScale chain before the physics->mass load; in ours the mass load outranks it because it is anti-dependent on the dead VU temp store
(`sqc2 vf3,0x10(sp)`) that has all later loads as successors, (3) abs.s/sub.s position before jal func_001FAC98, (4) swc1 order of desired[1]/[2].

# Wave-2 lane 5 notes

## func_001C9190 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001C9190.cpp), no pins, no asm  (was lane1.v4, aligned 5)
Both remaining diff groups of lane1.v4 had ONE root cause: pointer locals declared before the outer `for(;;)`
(`VIFS *builder = &D_002DEA28; CLOCK *clock = &D_002DDED0;`) create real insns in the entry block, so the loop header is NOT the first
basic block and gcse has a pre-header (bb0) to hoist into. Write the function so that it starts directly with the loop and use the
globals directly (`D_002DEA28.AddDmaCnt()`, `&D_002DDED0`): the loop header is then bb0 itself, gcse can no longer move the highs of the
outer-loop-head block (`lui` of D_003D3A3C / D_002DEDBC) out of the loop, they stay local pseudos, and:
  (1) local-alloc gives the `lui` after the int-returning WaitSema call to $v1 (hard reg v0 stays "live" after a call_value in local-alloc;
      the same effect is visible in the neighbour func_001C9098: `lui v1` after SignalSema), whereas a gcse-hoisted high is re-materialised by
      reload and gets $v0 (target: `lui v1,0x2E; b; sw zero,-0x1244(v1)`);
  (2) the other gcse-hoisted `high`s (DF260, 3A40, DEA4C ...) now stay inside the loops at loop.c time, so the inner loop has >= 96 insns in the
      second loop pass (`-da -dL`: "Loop from..: N real insns") and the `li 1` for `m_fCheckCnt = 1` is no longer hoisted (it stays a local pseudo,
      sched1 puts it early and local-alloc picks $a1), exactly as in the target. This was the earlier "need 3 more insns" mystery (I had worked
      around it with three empty volatile asm in an interim draft; not needed).
Also needed (already in lane1.v4): `for(;;){ BeginFrame; Poll; if(count) break; body }`, `-*D_002DD314 + frameT`, `do{ flags &= -2; }while(0)`;
and `uchar *pb = D_002DEA28.m_pb; D_002DEA4C->packet = pb;` (m_pb loaded before the frame pointer), real VIFS/GIFS classes from include/ for
AddDmaCnt/AddVifDirect/EndDmaCnt, OpenFrame() = func_00160828, WaitSema/SignalSema/PollSema = func_00234A60/40/90.
GENERAL LESSON: if a loop-heavy function has `lui` register swaps ($v1 vs $v0) and loop-hoisting differences at the loop head, check whether the
function starts with the loop. Pointer-to-global locals before the loop add a pre-header block that lets gcse PRE hoist every always-executed
`high` out of the loop (they become unallocated pseudos re-materialised by reload with $v0 and they shrink the loop's insn count for loop.c).
Reload facts I verified on the way (reload1.c order_regs_for_reload/allocate_reload_reg): reload takes the lowest call-used reg that is not
live before/after the insn and not used by a live pseudo; `last_spill_reg` rotates round-robin over the function-wide spill_regs list, so once
reg 3 is a spill reg every later single reload alternates $v0/$v1 (this ruled out "reload picks v1" as the explanation).

## func_001A4258 - near-miss, aligned 72 (build/hand_wip/func_001A4258.w2lane5.v1.cpp; was 81 for the best clean draft). Not matched.
Fresh rewrite from the asm with one asm per VU op group (helpers in the style of func_00151C38/func_00137248: Sub, Length=sq|sqrt, Dot, Cross,
Normalize=sq|sqrt|div, DivScalar, Combine, AddScaled, Scalar with a function-scope `U scalar` home for the dead `sqc2 vfN,0(sp)` stores, SqrtF).
Lessons (each measured, tool = build/lane_scratch/w2lane5/a4258/{mkv,ev,flips}.sh + opdiff.py: opcode-only alignment of the check output):
1. Plain `qword a = sp->p;` loads (movti into a "j" pseudo) give the target's `lqc2`; an `lqc2` asm instead makes sched1/2 treat the load as cost-1 asm and
   reorders lwc1/vsub around it. Keep loads plain.
2. A VU asm statement is a scheduling barrier only if `volatile`. The target's order is reproduced by a MIXED pattern of volatile and plain asm
   (here: Sub/Length-sqrt/Dot/DivScalar/Combine plain; Length-sq, Cross, Normalize*, AddScaled, sqrt.s volatile). Loop 1 (sphere test) then matches at the opcode
   level (lqc2, lwc1, vsub, sq, sqrt, add.s, qmfc2, mtc1, c.lt.s). Do a one-flag-at-a-time search over volatile/plain per asm group with an opcode-only diff metric
   (the positional count is too noisy): 12 flags, each round = 12 compiles (~4 min with xargs -P6 here; compile spawn is slow on this box).
3. Keep float extraction separate: `qword lenq = Length(axis); qword c1 = Cross(d,axis); float len = GetX(lenq);` (GetX = plain qmfc2 asm with a float "=r" output,
   gcc adds the mtc1 itself). Returning the qword first and converting later is what lets the cross sit between the sqrt and the qmfc2 as in the target.
4. sched facts (from -fsched-verbose-5): forward list scheduler; asm insns cost 1; tie-break prefers the insn that depends on the last scheduled insn, then
   more dependents, then lower LUID; a recognised memory op (lqc2 = [memory vu0], lwc1 = [memory]) occupies the memory unit for a cycle, which pushes lwc1 one cycle later.
Remaining diffs of the saved draft (13 opcode diffs, vf numbering not matching either: target uses vf1..vf10, we use vf1..vf8, because in the target the
four scalar vectors (s, root, ra, rb) stay live until their delayed dead sqc2 stores): (a) 0.0f is hoisted out of the pair loop (target loads it inside, 1.0f hoisted;
same loop.c life*95>=insn_count mechanism as func_001C9190, needs more insns in the loop), (b) rb lwc1 sits before the sqrt asm instead of between the
sq and sqrt asms, (c) the delayed sqc2/mfc1 order of the four Scalar temps, (d) the three final Dot products come in a different order and the first c.lt.s
is too early. Explicit placement of the `home.q = ...` stores after chosen asm (h1 experiment) was worse (dead-store elimination removes some).

## Agent w2lane6

# Wave-2 lane 6 notes (agent w2lane6)

## Tooling written (build/lane_scratch/w2lane6/)
- `la.py BASE [-x]`  (BASE = dump prefix such as `a1.src.cc`, made by `dump.sh SRC -da`): models gcc 2.95 **local-alloc** per basic block from the
  `.sched` + `.lreg` dumps (quantity priority = floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/(death-birth), ties -> lower qty number, first-fit lowest hard reg, ties of a
  dying input with the output of 2/3-operand insns). It reproduces the real local allocation of every call-free block I tested (prints mismatches).
  Conflict test must be INCLUSIVE (birth==death of two quantities conflicts: `lw v1,0(a2); lq v0,0(v1)`), despite the exclusive loop in find_free_reg's source.
  `lalloc.py BASE BLK` = same, one block, with the quantity table. `srch.py`/`ev.py` run a source variant through matchtool (+ dump + model).
- Real gcc-2.95.3 sources are readable via WebFetch of raw.githubusercontent.com/gcc-mirror/gcc/releases/gcc-2.95.3/gcc/local-alloc.c (ask for exact functions).

## Facts about local-alloc learned (apply to every register-swap near miss)
1. Allocation order inside a block is by the priority above, NOT by declaration order. Compute it with la.py before trying source variants.
2. A pseudo that is live across a basic-block boundary is NOT allocated by local-alloc (global-alloc does it afterwards, first-fit avoiding conflicts with the
   locals). That is why `lui v1,%hi(X); lw v1,%lo(X)(v1)` exists in the original (hi = local pseudo, loaded value = global pseudo; no conflict at the touching
   point). Seen in matched src/hand/func_001E8008.c (`switch (D_002C3650)`). In func_001BFF28 the same pair appears for the `D_002C0C04` bits load.
3. `ptr = base + off` ties `ptr` with `base` (first dying operand), `x = y + 1` ties x with y if y dies there: the order of the two insns in sched1
   decides whether `sll`/`addiu` share the register of the loaded count (func_001BFF28 loop 2: `int n = v->count; v->count = n + 1; e = v->edges + n;`
   gives the target's order `sll v1,v0,3 / addiu v0,v0,1`; `v->count++` after `e = ...` does not).
4. Splitting one VU distance asm into the 4 asm statements of the matched src/hand/func_001BF980.cpp `Dist2` (vsub / vmul+vaddy / vsqrt+vwaitq+vaddq / qmfc2) lengthens
   the live range of `e` (edge pointer) by two insns, which lowers its quantity priority below the `sll` temp: that fixed the v0/v1 swap in loop 1.
5. dbr (delay-slot filler) never moves the `sw cost` into the branch delay slot of loops 1/2 in the target (it takes the next iteration's first load instead).
   An empty non-volatile `asm("" : "+m"(e->cost))` after the store did that (lane3), but a `volatile int cost` member in EDGE does the same with NO asm and
   no extra refs on `e` (reg_n_refs of e matter for priority). I use the volatile member.
6. Dead `sqc2 vfN,0x10($sp)` of the scalar in the tail comes from passing the `VUF` struct by value to an inline helper; its position depends on source
   order of the arguments/lets.

## func_001BFF28 - near-miss, best clean-ish: build/hand_wip/func_001BFF28.w2lane6.v2.cpp (aligned 16, 19 raw diffs, ZERO pins);
## with ONE pin (`register int c asm("$4")` for loop-1 cost) v1: build/hand_wip/func_001BFF28.w2lane6.v1.cpp = aligned 6, 9 raw diffs, all in the tail
Starting point lane3.v1 (aligned 23). Changes that each helped (all measured): split VU asm (fact 4), `volatile int cost` in EDGE instead of the "+m" asm (fact 5),
loop 2 as `n = v->count; v->count = n+1; e = v->edges + n;` (fact 3). Loop 2 now has the exact target registers/order.
Remaining:
- loop 1: target allocates slti->v0, cost(qmfc2 result)->a0 (loop counter a1, pointer a3). By the model the qmfc2 result has the HIGHEST local priority (4 refs
  over 2 half-insns) so it always takes v0, forcing slti to a1. The target is consistent with the qmfc2 result being a GLOBAL pseudo (fact 2) -- allocated after the
  locals, first free reg = a0 -- but I found no natural source form that makes it cross a block boundary. The single pin on it fixes the loop.
- tail (`count == 1` block; 9 diffs): target has hi/bits both v1 (bits = global pseudo, fact 2), hi of D_002A2DA0 = v0, `qmtc2` before the `addiu`, and the dead
  `sqc2 vf3,0x10(sp)` right after the qmtc2/`addiu s0,2` (before the axis `lqc2`). Ours: bits->a0, hi->v0, address->v1, store last. Needs the sched1 order
  hi2(lui 2A) before hi1 (axis address first; `qword axis = D_002A2DA0;` first in the source gives hi1=v1/hi2=v0 but moves vf numbering), a global bits, and the scalar
  home store before the axis load. Dead ends: `extern const float/qword` (worse), int alias global, volatile load, float "r" operand (goes through FPR), do/while wrapper.

## Other functions: see sections below (appended as I go).

## CORRECTION to the allocation model (final, validated): la.py default SLACK=2
A quantity reserves its hard register until death+2 (one whole insn after its last use): a quantity BORN in the very next insn after another one's last use
CANNOT share its register (my first inclusive rule, slack 0, was only approximately right). With SLACK=2 `la.py` reproduces every call-free integer/VU block I tested
(a1, b1, c1 dumps: b1 block 5 and 11, a1 block 27 all exact); FP-class blocks and blocks with calls (call-crossing -> s-regs) are not modelled.
Eligibility for local-alloc (checked in local-alloc.c): REG_BASIC_BLOCK>=0 AND REG_N_DEATHS==1 (and class not likely-spilled); everything else (live across a block
boundary, or a user variable with 2+ death points) is a GLOBAL pseudo, allocated after all locals. Allocation-order facts: highest priority first = lowest free hard reg.
Usage: `bash dump.sh build/lane_scratch/w2lane6/X.cpp -da` then `./run.sh la.py X.src.cc` (per-block mismatch list) or `lalloc.py X.src.cc BLK` (quantity table).

## func_001A3D00 - not solved (kept lane5.v2, aligned 26)
With the validated model our block 5 (bit scan + slot store) is reproduced exactly: the tail pseudos (address = s+slot*4, tied pair, pri 60000) and `slot` (30000) are allocated
before `n` (28000) so they take v0/v1 and n ends in a0 (= i's reg). The target is the exact reverse (n=v0, slot=v1, address=a0), and its `addu a0,s1,a0` has the
pointer FIRST (ours `addu v0,v0,s1`). Priority alone cannot reverse that (n would need >=32 refs, or the tail pseudos <28000). So the target RTL differs in the tail
(different operand order of the commutative plus = different expression shape / cse quantity order). Tried: `s->x4154[slot]=o`, `(char*)s+0x4154+slot*4`, pointer var: all worse (aligned 28-36).
Also the barrier `volatile asm` of lane5.v2 makes sched1 keep ~b / old&~b late; the target has `nor` early (no barrier in the original).

- Root cause: the target loop is a REAL loop (NOTE_INSN_LOOP_BEG/END), so flow weights its refs x2 (n*4: 14 refs / 82 insns = .512, each Lerp
  pointer: 4 refs / 13 insns = .615) and the pointers are allocated BEFORE n*4. The goto-loop draft had depth 0 (.1707 vs .1538).
- But loop.c must NOT optimize it (a plain `for(;;)` gets the 1.0f and the %hi(D_002C3678) hoisted out; threshold = 2*95 = 190 insns without a call,
  95 with one; the loop is ~130 insns). Fix = the guide's "multiple entry" trick: test the first sample before the loop and `goto top;` into the first
  label inside `for(;;){ top: ... }` => loop_invalid, no -dL output for the big loop, byte-identical.
- NOTE: `if(!(t<x)) goto top;` gives c.le.s/bc1t (fast-math inverts), so keep `if(t<x){store; goto finished;} goto top;`; the `goto top;` is not
  deleted because the label is inside the loop notes.
- Loop shape that matched: for(;;){ top: if(n<cap){ n++; ...; if(time<X){store;break;} } else {active=0;break;} }  (cap test stays at the loop head;
  `for(;;){ if(n>=cap){..;break;} ...}` makes jump.c rotate the test to the bottom - wrong).

with lo/hi loaded first. GENERAL LESSON: a float obtained from a VU result should be a TI value moved by the compiler (movti VF->GPR) and consumed by an "r" mtc1 asm
(GetX), not `qmfc2` into an int in asm; this changes which pseudos local-alloc sees and where the GPR temps land.

REGISTER-LESS pseudos handled by reload (output reload shares the input reload reg), not of local-alloc (which would give vf1 to both). Something makes the Add result a spilled
pseudo; not found. Also the target materializes `-1` ($v1) in the block after the first compare BEFORE the lwc1 of D_002DDF0C; ours schedules lwc1 first.

## Summary of reusable lessons (wave 2 lane 7)
1. A "goto-loop" has loop depth 0; a real loop doubles every ref count in global/local alloc priorities (floor_log2(refs)*refs/len). When the target's priorities look like a loop but loop.c
   did not touch it (nothing hoisted/strength-reduced), keep the real `for(;;)` and make it multi-entry: test the first element before the loop, then `goto top;` to a label INSIDE the
   loop. (func_001E95B0)
2. gcc-2.95 stack slots: named locals at declaration; calls returning structs and by-value temps come from the temp pool, freed slots coalesce when adjacent (16B + 48B -> 64B at the
   same address), so an M3 return, a following M4 return and a VU_FLOAT arg temp can all share one address; a g++ named-return-value inline (`M3 f(..) return r {..}`) yields exactly
   {local copy, return temp} dead stores; a plain `qword x` with its address taken gets its slot lazily at the END of the frame, a struct/union gets a pool slot at its declaration.
3. Float from a VU register: keep the value as a TI qword and let the compiler emit qmfc2.ni; take the float with an "r" mtc1 asm (GetX). An int temp + two asms, or one combined
   asm, changes local-alloc order for all the address registers. (func_001D0190: 24 -> 9; then `time` before `distance` -> MATCH; func_001D09A0 41 -> 32)

## func_001C6DE8 - near-miss (build/hand_wip/func_001C6DE8.w2lane8.v1.cpp, aligned 3, no pins, no unions, no volatile; only the VU asm)
Big jump from 13 -> 3 (c1: 4) by using the BuildFrustrum idiom instead of unions/volatile asm:
`struct VU_FLOAT { qword q; VU_FLOAT(float f){ asm("qmtc2.ni %1,%0":"=j"(q):"r"(f)); } }; struct VU_VECTOR { qword q; VU_VECTOR(const qword&v){q=v;} };`
and `static inline qword AddScaled(VU_VECTOR a, VU_VECTOR b, VU_FLOAT s)` (one asm: vadda.xyzw ACC,a,vf0 / vmaddx.xyzw r,b,sx),
called as `upper = AddScaled(point, D_002A2DC0, hi); lower = AddScaled(point, D_002A2DC0, lo);`.
The by-value VU_FLOAT arg temp lives in a stack slot (addressable via the ctor's `this`), which is exactly the pair of dead
`sqc2 vf2/vf3,0x70(sp)` stores; sched1/sched2 then order lower-before-upper and put vf2=hi/vf3=lo as in the target.
No unions, no volatile needed. The arena is `struct ARENA{char pad[2];}` with explicit ctor call returning ARENA* (void return is worse: 11)
and `func_001AE4A0(&arena,2)` in 3 separate return blocks (as lane9).
Declaring `O **objects = objects0; int count = count0;` (objects first) as locals instead of using the params directly flips the
entry stores to `sw a2` before / `sw a1` in the jal delay slot (target order) -> 4 -> 3.
Remaining 3 diffs: (1) target has `sw a2,0x84` BEFORE `sd ra`/`swc1 f20`, ours after them (in sched2 the prologue saves have lower luid; the
target's sw a2 must outrank them: prio 13 vs 12, or one more dependent); (2) target keeps `sqc2 vf3,0x70` before `bnez` and fills the bnez delay slot with
`lq a2,0xA0` from the fall-through; ours puts the sqc2 in the delay slot (reorg did not accept the last store as filler in the target).
Dead ends: FRAME class with ctor/dtor (frame 0x100, wrong), ctor declared void (11), const/pure attribute on ctor (no change).

Final steps after the 17: (1) the mtc1 asm got an extra UNUSED "j"(r) input (allocation-only): r (the vmsubw result) was classed TI_REGS (GPR) by regclass
because its uses were GPR-ish (asm "r" operand + sq store); the extra VU use keeps it in a VU reg => product=vf1/unit=vf2/pos-reload=vf4 and `sqc2 vf1` dot store: 17 -> 3.
(2) func_001A7C18 declared with the float in the MIDDLE of the argument list (before the second qword): arg evaluation order moves `mov.s $f12` to the target position: 3 -> 0 (aligned).
(3) The last 5 diffs were only the spill-slot offsets 0xD0/0xD4 (&normal vs i+1). Cause found in the .gcse dump: both are gcse PRE "reaching regs" (224=i+1, 225=index<<4,
226=at+80); PRE creates them in EXPRESSION HASH-TABLE ORDER (bucket order), reload then assigns spill slots in ascending pseudo number. hash(plus (reg r) (const c)) = 45+r+c
mod S with S=(n_insns/2)|1=73 here: hash(at+80)=53, hash(i+1)=25 (r=125). Needed hash(at+80) < hash(i+1): regno of `i` in [154,172], or table size >= 87.
30 unused locals declared before `i` (shifts its pseudo number) => MATCH. (Any 29..47 work.) Check "Expression hash table" + "PRE: redundant insn .. reaching reg is N" in the .gcse dump.

## func_001BFDB0 - not matched (stays at the old draft; best natural attempt aligned 48, old 2-pin draft 20)
New findings: gcse DOES copy-propagate plain `aa=a; cc=c`; cse1 merges any reload of *t->a / *t->c in the final block (all those variants score the same 48). Best theory for the target's vmoves
(vmove vf8,vf6 right after the first cross, vmove vf7,vf3 as the first bc1f delay-slot filler): they are gcse PRE copies. The .gcse dump of the target-like source would have to show
"PRE: redundant insn N ... reaching reg is R" for the loads; PRE runs once ("1 pass") and cprop is not run after it, so reaching-reg copies survive to the end. I could not find a source form where cse1 leaves
the second load for gcse (asm_operands are also gcse expressions - non-volatile asm with identical inputs is PRE'd/cse'd just like a load).

## func_001C6DE8 - extra negative results
Tried: float_ctor via a function returning VU_FLOAT, AddScaled taking const VU_FLOAT&, `objects == 0`, lower-before-upper (worse, 5). None changes the 3 remaining diffs.
Analysis of the sched2 table: the target order [sw a2, sd ra, swc1 f20, jal; sw a1 in the delay slot] is what you get when BOTH param-home stores have priority 13 (> the two prologue saves, which have 12) and
sw a1 is then pulled into the delay slot by reorg (the frame-related swc1/sd saves are not usable as fillers). Ours: both 12, saves first by luid. Need one extra true/anti dependent on each store.

## Lessons (for the coordinator / BRIEF2)
1. VU class steering: for `mtc1 %1,%0 : "=f"(f) : "r"(q)` add an unused `"j"(q_vf_pseudo)` input - without it regclass puts the asm result pseudo in a GPR pair (store becomes `sq`, vf numbering shifts).
   87F0: 17 -> 3 aligned; 8B50: three PlaneDistance expansions fixed.
2. gcse PRE reaching registers are numbered in expression-hash-bucket order; reload then hands out spill slots in ascending pseudo number. hash(plus (reg r) (const c)) = (2236 - r_base + r + c) mod S
   (at=reg 1: c+2236; user reg r: r+c+2235), S=(n_insns/2)|1 (see "Expression hash table (S buckets" in the .gcse dump). If two hoisted/spilled temporaries have swapped slots, shifting the regno of one operand
   (unused locals declared earlier) or the insn count flips the order. Matched func_001A87F0 this way.
3. Pseudo number decides cse1's canonical register of an equivalence class (lowest regno), hence whether a loop guard tests the original (`beqz head`) or the loop variable: declare the loop variable at
   function scope (low regno) or inside the loop after the source variable (high regno) to select.
4. A temporary that must be live across a call (`qword pt = m->point;` before a ctor call) is a callee-saved GPR and thereby decides the s-register assignment of the loop pointers.
5. Reading a value BEFORE the branch that conditionally uses it (`qword plane = s->plane; if (...)`) lets reorg fill the delay slot from before (no annulled bnel).
6. Put a float parameter at a different position in a callee prototype (EABI: no change in callee) to change argument-evaluation order and so the order of the arg moves before a call.

# w3lane1 notes (wave 3, lane 1)

Scratch: build/lane_scratch/w3lane1 (dump.sh = -da dumps into a dir, ck.sh = matchtool summary, an.sh = check + global-alloc
priority list (prio2.py) + gcse PRE lines; base.c/base2.c + vars*.py = variant generators for 9C88, c*.py for 6DE8).

## func_001E9C88 - not matched (best stays build/hand_wip/func_001E9C88.lane8.v1.c, aligned 2)

Root cause found (pass = local-alloc update_equiv_regs, fed by cse2):
* update_equiv_regs DOUBLES REG_LIVE_LENGTH of a pseudo whose FIRST set (in insn order) has a function-invariant REG_EQUAL
  note, even when the pseudo is set more than once; the later set calls no_equiv(), which deletes the note but not the
  doubling (gcc 2.95.3 local-alloc.c). This applies to every `x = 0` init too: cpv, pseg, cseg, outside all have doubled
  lengths in the .lreg dump (flow 124 -> lreg 218 for cseg).
* Store-first `q[cpv++] = pCur` makes loop.c's giv init a plain copy (`ppv = q`); cse2 rewrites it to `ppv = P` (P = gcse's
  hoisted &apv) and adds REG_EQUAL (plus at 512) because P/q are in the same cse block (bb0 + preheader, no label between).
  => ppv length 59 -> 118, priority 2*7/118 = .1186 < cseg .1927 => s6/s7/fp rotation. Increment-first (lane8 v1) has init
  (plus at 508), which is a NEW value for cse2 => no note, no doubling, right allocation, but the init offset / store order differ.
* P (the hoisted &apv for the final PvAllocCopy call) cannot be avoided: 2.95's LCM (pre_lcm) hoists ANY expression that is
  anticipated at a loop entry to the end of the block before the loop (lone after-loop occurrence included: tested with all
  in-loop &apv SETs removed via `*(apv + i)` addressing, which puts 512 in the MEM displacement - P is still created). And P must
  be REG_EQUIV (target frame 0x3F0, `addiu a1,sp,0x200` rematerialised), which needs an earlier provider pseudo (q) in bb0 -
  which in turn guarantees the note on the preheader init. So with this source shape the target needs ppv >= 8 weighted refs
  (3*8/118 = .203 > cseg), i.e. one more ref at loop depth 1, or the store at depth 2.
* Verified: `do { q[cpv++] = pCur; } while (0);` (APPEND-macro style) gives ppv 10 refs (.254 > cseg) and the right store/
  increment order (sw, cpv, ppv), but cpv also gets 18 refs (.3077 > pseg .2778 -> s4/s5 swap) and the fake loop's LOOP_BEG
  makes shorten_branches align the next label (0x1E9DBC, not 8-aligned -> extra nop). In 2.95.3 final.c the label search after
  a LOOP_BEG note has no limit and does not stop at LOOP_END. w2lane2's store-only do-while has the nop + inc-order problem.
* Dead ends this round (all measured): -fno-gcse oracle (hoists the %hi lui, no &pos2 hoist: aligned 35-45); pointer-arith
  `*(apv+i)` search/store (25); inline FFind/AppendPv helpers (= base forms, 23-59); user ppv inside an explicit `if (t<tEnd)`
  guard with do-while or for (51-65: PRE inserts at bb0 AND bb1, ppv still doubled and spilled); q only for the final call (25);
  do-while(0) around the whole loop (45) or the whole search+insert (59). Compiling the draft as C++ gives identical results.
* Open: a natural construct that adds one depth-1 reference to the giv register (or keeps the preheader init out of the cse2
  block that contains P) without a LOOP note.

## func_001C6DE8 - not matched (best stays build/hand_wip/func_001C6DE8.w2lane8.v1.cpp, aligned 3)

Diff 1 (sched2, block 0 = prologue + body up to the first bnez; the arena ctor call is inside the block):
* Ready list at t=8: 26 (`sw a2,0x84`), 487 (`sd ra`), 495 (`swc1 f20`), 29 (`sw a1,0x80`) all prio 12, 2 dependents each
  (the call via flush_pending_lists, and the block-end jump); tie goes to luid, and the prologue is always inserted before
  every body insn, so 487/495 win. Target needs 26 at prio 13 (it would then sit right after the prio-13 f23/f22/f21 saves) or
  3 dependents. Nothing visible in the target writes a2 or touches 0x84 before the call, and the call flushes all memory
  dependences (anti, cost 1), so I found no source mechanism. Params used directly (address-taken) store a1 first (worse, 4);
  count-param/objects-local copy = 3; declaration orders, QUERY {count, objects} struct like func_00202378 (moves the slot to
  0x60, prologue order unchanged), RAII arena with char pad[2] (frame 0x100), upper/lower as U unions or VU_VECTOR objects
  (19-20), `qword upper, lower;` declared together (point slot moves) - none better.
Diff 2 (reorg): fill_simple_delay_slots takes the last insn before bnez (the gcc-generated `sqc2 vf3,0x70` VU_FLOAT temp
  store, insn/i from the inlined ctor). Target keeps it and has `lq a2,0xA0` (the FindSwObjects upper arg) in the slot, so in
  the target either that store was not a fill candidate (an asm stops stop_search_p) and block 1 was scheduled with the a2
  load first, or the a2 load was the last insn of block 0. sched1 regions are all single-block here (no interblock motion).
  Diagnostic asm/volatile versions of the temp store change the frame (0x120) - not a lead.

## General lessons
1. Any pseudo whose first set has an invariant REG_EQUAL note (including `= 0` inits!) gets its live length doubled in
   local-alloc even if it is set again later; compare .flow vs .lreg "across N insns" to see it. A giv init that is a copy of an
   fp-relative pseudo (cse2 adds REG_EQUAL (plus at K)) halves the giv's priority; an init value that is new to cse2 does not.
2. 2.95's gcse hoists every anticipated expression (even a single after-loop occurrence) to the end of the block before the
   first loop; `*(arr + i)` (pointer arithmetic) instead of `arr[i]` moves the array offset into the MEM displacement and removes
   the in-loop `t = fp + K` SETs from gcse's table (useful when an in-loop PRE copy is the problem).
3. sched2 ties after reload: priority, then (no reg-weight after reload) dependents count, then luid; prologue insns always have
   the lowest luids, so a body store can only precede a prologue save of equal priority through an extra dependent.

## Agent w3lane2

# Wave 3 lane 2 notes (agent w3lane2)

## func_001DF0A0 -> MATCHED (src/hand/func_001DF0A0.cpp), NO pins
(A first C match with the lane4 stride pin is kept as build/hand_wip/func_001DF0A0.w3lane2.v1.c.)
Started from lane4 v1 (aligned 8). Four independent causes, each found from the dumps:
1. **Prologue `daddu s5,a1` vs `sq s4`** (sched2 tie): the function has the same 6-argument virtual signature as matched
   func_001823C8 / func_0013ADE8, `(OBJ *p, REQUEST *q, int, int, int, CONTACT **)`, and calls the empty base stub
   `func_001F5990(p, q, a2, a3, a4, pout)` with every argument passed through. No instructions are emitted for that,
   but it fixes the prologue order (8 -> 7 at once). Earlier lanes had declared the stub `void(void)`.
2. **v0/v1 swap (&e->head in v1, distance scratch in v0)**: the distance is a FLOAT in a GPR,
   `float dist = Scalar(len)` with `Scalar = asm("qmfc2.ni %0,%1" : "=r"(f) : "j"(v))`, passed as PxpAdd's int argument
   with `*(int *)&dist` (addressof -> (subreg:SI (reg:SF))). local-alloc does not tie SF to SI (MODES_TIEABLE_P), so the
   float pseudo gets no t2 suggestion and takes v0 by priority before &e->head (-> v1); then `daddu t2,v0`.
   The old `long` param / (long)qword gave a reload through v1 instead, and an int asm output was tied straight to t2.
3. **VU order + vf numbers**: Len split into a dot asm (outputs d and an allocated `one`) and a sqrt asm gives the
   target order (B1 A2 S1 B2 Q1 S2); the register numbers came out right only with a **two-input Dot(a, b)**
   (`vmul.xyz %0,%2,%3`) and `Len(v) = Sqrt(Dot(v, v))`. Reason (verified with the local-alloc priority formula,
   floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/life, asm outputs tied to a dying input): each input of a 2-output asm is counted once per
   output SET, so the second operand adds 4 weighted refs to d0 and to out1/dir; dir's qty (out1,dir) then outranks the
   two `one` temps (priority 58181 vs 40000), takes vf2, the `one`s take vf3 and dot2/sqrt2 vf4, out0 vf4 = target.
4. **Stride pin removed**: the source is C++ — D_002A7AC0 is the constant pointer-to-member `&OBJ::Notify`
   (`void Notify() __asm__("func_001DF260")`, PMF {0,-1,func_001DF260}, as in matched func_001BAED8), and the VU helpers
   take `VU_VECTOR` parameters (`struct VU_VECTOR { qword q; VU_VECTOR(const qword &v) { q = v; } };`, the class from
   func_001C3500) while returning plain qwords. The conversion copies survive cse, so the loop has 123/116 real insns
   (was 86/78 with qword params); above ~95 loop.c leaves `li 0x150` in the loop, i*0x150 is no giv, and the target's
   in-loop `mult` appears with `ENTRY *e = &p->entries[i]`. Returning VU_VECTOR too is wrong (values go through a stack
   temp at 0xA0). In plain C, struct wrappers add nothing (86/78) and a by-value PAIR is fine, but in C++ a PAIR struct
   argument is copied to the stack (sdl/sdr) - use the PMF.

## func_001999D0 -> MATCHED (src/hand/func_001999D0.cpp), NO pins, no allocation-only operands, no barriers
Fresh rewrite (structs from w2lane3 v3), using the idioms of matched func_001C3500 / func_001DF0A0:
1. Block-scoped locals per case (`EDGE *edges[64]; U direction;` in case 1, `U direction; SPAN spans[2];` in case 2) give the
   frame exactly (case 2 reuses the freed case-1 slots); kind 2's `sum` is a plain qword local that reload SPILLS (crosses 4
   calls, caller-save not profitable) -> the 0x110 slot, the per-iteration lqc2/vadd/sqc2, and the two separate reloads
   (Len input vf4, Div input vf2) all come from reload, no `"+m"` barrier needed; kind 1's sum stays in vf3 with caller-save
   (0x120) once Dot has two inputs (`Len(v) = Sqrt(Dot(v, v))`, which also raises the refs past 4*calls).
2. **Return-style Normalize** (`l = Len(v); if (X(l) < 1e-4f) return fallback; return Div(v, l);`), as in func_001C3500:
   the fallback is loaded into a GPR before the branch and moved with gcc's own qmtc2 on the fallback path; the result is
   vf1 on both paths and both kinds' fallback tails cross-jump. An `if/else` assigning one variable lets jump.c hoist
   `r = fallback` above the branch (that is why every earlier draft needed `register qword res asm("vf1")`).
3. **X(l) = asm("mtc1 %1,%0" : "=f" : "r"(q))** for the compare, not a qmfc2 asm + compiler mtc1: reorg's backward
   delay-slot search STOPS at an asm insn (stop_search_p), so in kind 2 it cannot reach the fallback `lq v0` and fills the
   bc1f slot from the divide block (the `lqc2 vf2,0x110(sp)` reload) as in the target; in kind 1 the lq is scheduled after
   the asm and still lands in the bc1t slot. This was the last 1-instruction difference.

## General lessons (both functions)
- **reorg stops its backward delay-slot search at any asm insn** (stop_search_p). Whether a compare helper is an asm
  (`X()` = mtc1 asm) or plain C decides which insn fills the following branch's delay slot.
- **Return-style inline (`if (c) return fallback; return Div(v, l);`)** keeps the fallback copy on its own path; an
  if/else assigning one variable lets jump.c hoist the cheap copy above the branch (the usual reason for vf pins).
- **Two-input `Dot(a, b)`** with `Len(v) = Sqrt(Dot(v, v))`: inputs of a 2-output asm count once per output SET, so the
  repeated operand is a natural source of the extra refs earlier lanes added as "allocation-only" duplicates.
- **Pass-through base call**: a stub called with all of the caller's arguments emits nothing but its USEs change sched2
  ties in the prologue; check sibling callers of the stub for its real arity.
- **Float bits to an int argument**: `float f` from a qmfc2 "=r" asm + `*(int *)&f` (SF->SI not tieable in local-alloc,
  so the float takes the lowest free GPR instead of the argument register).
- **VU_VECTOR(const qword &) parameters** on the VU helpers are the natural way to push a loop over loop.c's ~95-insn
  limit (no pin needed for an in-loop `mult`).
Scratch: build/lane_scratch/w3lane2 (dump.sh, chk.sh, lc.sh = loop insn counts, laq.py/blk.py = local-alloc views).

## func_00194F70 - MATCHED: src/hand/func_00194F70.cpp (one commented register pin, no asm, no goto)
Steps (each measured): w2lane1.v1 (12) -> plain locals instead of MERGE_WORK (8) -> natural inner search loop (6) -> pin (MATCH),
then cleanup: real names, trivial accessor inlines removed, `goto store` replaced by two plain stores, sizeof(JTGRABCAND) where
it is a plain size (the found-path memmove must stay `(ccand - j) * size - size`; `(ccand - j - 1) * size` is a different RTL, 11).
- Inner search: `j = ccand; for (;;) { if (--j < 0) { not-found: ...; break; } pcand = &ajtgrabcand[j]; if (same plo) { found...; break; } }`.
  The rolled exit test then contains the not-found CALLs, so jump.c's duplicate_loop_exit_test refuses it (target `daddu s3,s5; b test`),
  the loop keeps its LOOP_BEG (alignment nop) and that note, sitting right before the `goto test`, is a sched barrier that keeps the
  gcse-PRE-inserted `fp = i - 1` last (in the b delay slot). This replaced w2lane1's goto/do{}while(0) construction.
- The pin `register LO *plo asm("$6")` on the func_001956D0 result: global-alloc's copy-preference scan takes the lowest hard reg (v0);
  the original has it in a2 (and with it the a2 arg move first). 32 natural spellings tried first (gen8.py), none worked.
- Old notes: build/hand_wip/func_00194F70.w3lane3.v1.cpp: aligned 8 (from 12). Replacing the draft's MERGE_WORK stack struct by plain locals
  (`float bounds[2]; qword point; E *owned = &p->ae[0];` and the pq param used directly) turns pq/owned into spilled pseudos
  (slots 0x20/0x24 unchanged) and shifts reload's spill-register round robin: the owned temp and the final `lui` now use v1 as in the target.
- Loop 2 (a2 move before a0 + link result in a2): diagnostic asm copy `t` of the entry, defined BEFORE the `a->xc == 1` test and reused
  for the call result, reproduces both (t conflicts with the xc temp's v0 so global-alloc takes its a2 copy preference; its first value
  dies at the arg move so sched1 weight puts the a2 move first) - but then the separate `a` copy (s2) disappears. 32 natural loop-2
  spellings (gen8.py: block pointer vs a, &ae[j] arg, store/test orders, cond order, copy forms) none below 8.

## func_00195368 - near-miss: build/hand_wip/func_00195368.w3lane3.v2.cpp aligned 9 (was 11 with 3 allocation-only operands)
Why the old w2lane2.v3 (aligned 11) had its pointer inits BEFORE `addiu s0,-1; bltz`:
- In the target the work-buffer addresses `sp+0x1B0/0x1C0` stay inside the inner loop. gcse PRE (block-end insertion) hoists them into the
  loop preheader whenever the edge into the loop head is NOT critical. With the rotated `for(n--;n>=0;n--)` loop the edge (copied exit
  test -> body) is critical, so nothing is hoisted, but every user pointer init sits before the copied test. An explicit guard
  `n--; if(n>=0){ inits; do{...}while(--n>=0); }` puts the inits after the bltz (target order) but makes the edge non-critical, so PRE
  hoists the work addresses (w2lane2's b20a/b21, aligned 36-41).
- Fix: route the virtual sample call through an inline wrapper `SampleSource(SOURCE*, WORK *pw)`; the inliner substitutes the
  FIXED_BASE_PLUS const-equivalence of `pw` directly into the hard-reg argument moves, so gcse never sees a pseudo to hoist.
  Guard + do-while + wrapper (c2.c) = aligned 12 with ZERO allocation-only operands (the old draft needed three).
- `func_0011D448(r->lo, r->hi)` through an inline `RandomRange(RANGE*)` reproduces "lo via spec+0x50, hi via 4(ptr)" naturally
  (cse find_best_addr folds a REG address to the equal-cost PLUS form but not REG+4), and makes the two RANGE pointers loop.c
  movables instead of user variables (g-series).
- Giv model (indexing ages[produced] etc.) re-checked: loop.c always combines ages/ends (constant 0x30 apart) and makes the velocity
  array the combined base (later insn wins the benefit tie); outer loop pass 2 hoists every pass-1 giv-init base (life 3). Dead end.
Remaining in c2 (12): (1) produced s3 / ageOut s4 (global-alloc: produced 4*16/103=.6214 vs ageOut 3*11/54=.6111, endOut .5789);
(2) inner preheader sched1/local-alloc order. Model (simblk.py, la.py rules) says the target allocation (sll4->v0, base16->v1,
base0x180->a0, sll2->a1) needs sched1 to issue the 0x10 and 0x180 bases before the `sll 4`, impossible with user-pointer RTL because
`vectors` is at frame offset 0 (instantiate puts the base insn right before the add, after the shift).
Saved: build/hand_wip/func_00195368.w3lane3.v1.c (= c2, aligned 12, clean).

### Breakthrough: the target pointers are loop.c givs reduced in DIFFERENT loop passes
- Loop (for(n--;n>=0;n--)) with RandomRange helper, `{float*pa=ages+produced;*pa=age;}`, `ends[produced]=...`,
  `{U*pv=vectors+produced;pv[0]=origin;pv[10]=vel;}` (g5_A3E1O2_*.c). `ages+produced` as a VALUE expands base-first
  (base life 2) while `ends[produced]` / the vsv+0 vectors base have life 1. If the inner loop has 93..98 real insns in loop
  pass 1, only the life-2 ages base is moved => only ages is strength-reduced in pass 1 (its giv-init base then gets hoisted
  by outer pass 2 = the target's spilled 0x200 pseudo); ends and origins are reduced in pass 2 (bases stay in the inner
  preheader, never combined with ages). pv used twice makes the origin DEST_REG giv the combined base (velocity at +0xA0).
  The inner preheader then matches the target exactly (no sched/local-alloc tuning needed) and spill slots match.
- Shown with 20..25 diagnostic empty asm("") in the loop body (N=0: 73 insns -> fails; N=20..25 -> aligned 12; 26+ fails).
  Remaining with that probe: produced/ageOut s3/s4 swap + loop-tail order (the asm barriers). Need ~20-25 RTL insns of
  natural inflation (VU class pass-throughs etc.) that vanish from final code.
- Natural C++ form found (build/hand_wip/func_00195368.w3lane3.v2.cpp, aligned 9): VU_FLOAT ctor (qmtc2 asm), VU_VECTOR class,
  `VU_VECTOR vs = s * v; vScaled.q = vs.q;`, GRand(LM*), sample vectors/VU_FLOAT as block locals inside the inner loop and an
  `EMITX emitx` local in the submit block (gcc 2.95 frees block-scoped slots, so emitx reuses 0x1B0 - no union needed).
  Inner pass-1 count 91 (window ~91..95; the matched sibling helper idioms (=j outputs, no dup operand) drop it to 85-89).
- LAST diff = produced s3 / ageOut s4: produced 4*16/103=.6214 vs ageOut 3*11/54=.6111. Probe: 2..7 empty asm("") anywhere
  produced is live but the inner pointers are not (after `if(random<amount)n++;`, at the end of the outer body, at `submit:`)
  gives MATCH (build/lane_scratch/w3lane3/PROBE_match_with_2_empty_asm.cpp, NOT policy-clean). So the original has >=2 more
  RTL insns at sched1 time in the outer region that vanish later (e.g. spilled constant-equivalent pseudo defs such as the
  loop-hoisted &D_002DDED0 lui/addiu in the outer preheader, which reload deletes and rematerialises). Not found yet.
  Tried without effect: decl/init order of produced/i, for-init commas, if(!=-1){} instead of continue, while/for test forms,
  const-ref / by-value GRand helpers for the 0..1 random, sizeof-based 10, copies of n/psource (cse removes them).
- More dead ends for the last swap (all stay at 9 or get worse): FLess-style int compare helper (fixes the allocation through its
  extra outer-body RTL but leaves `n += flag` code, aligned 8), probabilistic-rounding inline helper (splits n, 27), local copy of
  produced around the inner loop (28), pin on produced (hard-reg var stops loop.c: 50), clock via pointer, renamed globals,
  EMITX ctor / pointer / field order, `if (produced <= 0) return;`, do{}while(0) around the count or the memset, statement orders
  of the count computation (o2/o3 identical code), -fmove-all-movables (diagnostic, 61).
- Second open issue in v2: operator* / AddScaled still carry the old duplicated "j" input (allocation-only) that gives the vf1/vf2
  numbering; with the matched sibling idioms (`"=j"(r)` outputs, no duplicate) the scalar gets vf1 because sched1 issues the
  `lqc2 v` before the qmtc2 (the GRand result's SF copy delays the qmtc2 by one insn); the loop-count window must then be re-found.
  Theory for both issues: the original's qmtc2 input has no separate SF call-value copy (that would remove 2 inner-loop insns, flip
  the lqc2/qmtc2 order and also flip produced/ageOut: 64/101 < 33/52) - no natural C form found that does it.
- Files: build/hand_wip/func_00195368.w3lane3.v1.c (guard form, 12), build/hand_wip/func_00195368.w3lane3.v2.cpp (C++ giv form, 9).

## Reusable lessons
1. Loop-test duplication: a rolled exit test that contains calls (e.g. the "not found" handling inside `if (--j < 0) {...; break;}`)
   is never copied in front of the loop by jump.c, which gives the target's `b test` loop entry with a real LOOP_BEG (alignment nop)
   instead of goto/do-while(0) tricks; the LOOP_BEG note then also fences sched around the jump.
2. A stack struct that bundles a parameter and a pointer (MERGE_WORK) changes reload's spill-register round robin; the plain
   parameter / local pointer (spilled pseudos at the same slots) reproduced the target's v1/v0 choices.
3. Strength reduction can happen in loop pass 1 or 2 per giv: a giv whose invariant base insn has life 1 is not moved (and not
   reduced) in pass 1 when the loop has > ~92 insns, but is in pass 2; pass-1 giv-init bases get hoisted by the outer pass 2,
   pass-2 ones do not. `p + i` (value) expands base-first (life 2) while `a[i]` expands shift-first (life 1).
4. gcse PRE never hoists into a loop whose entry edge is critical (copied exit test); an explicit `if` guard makes it
   non-critical. Passing `&local` through an inline wrapper puts the address straight into the hard argument register.

## Agent w3lane4

# Wave-3 lane 4 notes (agent w3lane4)

## func_001BFF28 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001BFF28.cpp), ONE commented pin (loop-1 cost in $4), no other hacks
Started from w2lane6.v1 (aligned 6, 9 raw diffs, all in the `count == 1` tail). The tail fix, step by step (all measured):
1. `VU_FLOAT(const float *pg)` with `"r,V"(*(const int *)pg)` (the func_0014F110 idiom): the word stays a MEM until reload, so the
   `lw` is an input reload that reuses the dying %hi register (`lui v1; lw v1,%lo(v1)` - NOT a global pseudo, as w2lane6 had guessed).
   Registers right, order wrong (lw after the out[1] store): 6 -> 4.
2. Construct the VU_FLOAT before `out[1].q = p;` (the read must precede the store in RTL; param-based store vs global read may alias):
   order now exact, only %hi(D_002C0C04)/%hi(D_002A2DA0) = v0/v1 swapped (aligned 5).
3. `extern const float D_002C0C04;` + ctor `VU_FLOAT(const float f)` with `"r,V"(f)`: MATCH. Mechanism (from the -da/-fsched-verbose-5 dumps):
   integrate.c substitutes the actual argument MEM for a TREE_READONLY parameter, and the const global's DECL_RTL MEM is RTX_UNCHANGING_P, so
   the asm's MEM input has no anti-dependence on the following out[1] store. sched1 priority of the qmtc2 asm drops 7 -> 4 (an asm insn's
   cost to every successor is 1, even for anti deps), so `lui %hi(D_002A2DA0)` (prio 6) is issued before `lui %hi(D_002C0C04)`; the C04 hi
   quantity then lives 2 insns (local-alloc priority 5000 < 5714 of the tied D_002A2DA0 hi/lo pair), so the pair gets v0 and the C04 hi v1.

## func_001BFDB0 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001BFDB0.cpp), no pins, VU asm only

1. The vmove copies (vf8 = a, vf7 = c, used only by the inside-triangle block): `struct VU_VECTOR { qword q; VU_VECTOR(const qword &v) { q = v; } };`

   Dead ends checked first: plain `qword aa = a` copies (top or inside: cse1 / gcse cprop propagate them; with cse's path broken cse makes the
   copy canonical and cprop reverts every use), reloading *pa in the inside block + a cse path break (do{}while(0) LOOP_END note): the
   reload survives cse1 but 2.95's PRE (lcm.c pre_lcm) does NOT delete it - its EARLY/LATE sets use only ANTLOC/TRANSP, no availability, so a
   computation in the entry block whose operands are set there (all param-derived values) never makes a later one redundant. So the w2lane8
   "gcse PRE copies" theory is wrong for this compiler. Union copies (U ua; ua.q = a), by-value VV ctor, array copies: no copies (aligned 48-49).
2. GPR choices: the z words of the three edge tests are output reloads of the `"=r"(float)` asm (pref FP_REGS pseudos), so their GPR is reload's
   round-robin over spill_regs (sorted by regno). With the old Read()+ExtractZ (TI GPR pseudo) form the inside block needed a third spill reg
   (a0), giving v1/a0/v0 instead of the target's v1/v0/v1. `ZOf`: `asm("pextuw %0,$zero,%1" : "=r"(z) : "r"(n))` (gcc emits the qmfc2 as an
   input reload): 14 -> 3. `XOf`: `asm("mtc1 %1,%0" : "=f"(x) : "r"(dv))` for the dot product (mtc1 now the asm, qmfc2 the reload; sched2 then
   orders mtc1 before the axis lqc2, removing the FPU-hazard nop before div.s): 3 -> MATCH.
Also tried for the func_001BFF28 pin after the BFDB0 work: asm "=r" output straight into the (volatile or plain) cost MEM, via an inline
reference/pointer parameter or a local `int *pc`: gcc 2.95 routes it through a stack temp (sw 0x10(sp) + copy, frame grows) - dead end.

## General lessons (wave 3, lane 4)
1. A GPR that "looks like a global pseudo" next to a dying %hi register (`lui v1; lw v1,%lo(v1)`) is usually a RELOAD: keep the word a MEM
   until reload with a never-chosen "V" alternative ("r,V"), and pass it by value as a `const` parameter of a `const` global so integrate.c
   substitutes the unchanging MEM (no anti-dependence on later stores -> different sched1 priority).
2. Float-in-GPR asm outputs (`"=r"(float)`, pref FP_REGS) are output reloads; their GPR comes from reload's round-robin over spill_regs (sorted
   by regno). Count how many distinct spill regs the whole function needs: one extra reload elsewhere (here a TI GPR pseudo from a
   qmfc2-into-qword helper) shifts every later choice. Prefer "r" inputs on pextuw/mtc1 asms so gcc emits the qmfc2 itself.
3. Surviving vmove copies: a struct with a `const qword &` conversion ctor (`VU_VECTOR va(a);`) keeps a TI copy alive through cse1 and gcse
   cprop; plain copies never survive. 2.95's PRE cannot create such copies for straight-line redundancy (its LCM ignores availability).
4. gcse expression-table size S = (insns/2)|1 picks PRE reaching-reg (and so spill-slot) order; by-value VU-class helper parameters add
   insns that survive cse1 and vanish later - a natural way to move S (func_001A87F0, replaces 30 padding locals).

## func_001DB0C8 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001DB0C8.cpp), 0 pins, no allocation-only operands
Start: w2lane7.v1 (aligned 24). Three source changes, each found from the dumps:
1. **Loop VF numbering (24 -> 14): the Add's ray operand must reach reload as a MEMORY operand.** In the target both Add results equal
   the ray reload reg (vf2 then vf3) and origin is vf1: that is reload's `combine_reloads` merging the input reload of a MEM operand with
   the output reload (combine had already turned the asm output into the call-argument hard reg a2). Our ray was loaded into a local
   pseudo first (expand_asm_operands force_regs a MEM for a "j"-only operand), local-alloc gave it vf1 and pushed origin to vf2.
   First found with `"jV"` on the Add input (V keeps the MEM until reload), later replaced by the natural form (3).
2. **Top block (`sw mode; li -1; lwc1 clock` order, -1 in v1):** sched1 had no dependence between the store to p->mode and the load of
   D_002DDF0C because alias.c `fixed_scalar_and_varying_struct_p` says a scalar at a fixed address cannot alias a struct member at a
   varying address. Reading it as a struct field (`extern CLOCK D_002DDED0; ... D_002DDED0.t3C`, offset 0x3C) makes both "struct" refs,
   the load then waits 2 cycles for the store, the `li -1` lands in between and overlaps the %hi pseudo -> v1. (aligned 16 -> 13)
3. **Splat `"rV"(*(const int*)&f)`** (the func_0012D220 idiom): target `lui v0; daddu s6,v0; lw v0,0x3274(v0)` = reload loading the
   MEM operand through the dying %hi reg (13 -> 5).
4. **Last 5 (count load scheduled early in the setup block; 2nd Add reload reg vf2 instead of vf3): `qword ray` as a plain local with
   `Add(qword a, const qword &b)` and a plain "j".** The reference binding makes ray ADDRESSOF during cse1 (the MEM is invalidated by the
   calls, so the two Adds stay separate), then purge_addressof turns ray back into a pseudo; global-alloc leaves it unallocated
   (refs 5 < 4*calls), so its def store `sqc2 vf3,0x80` and both loop loads are reload-generated. The store therefore doesn't exist in
   sched1 and in sched2 sits right after the vmulx, before the count load (which then waits on it), and reload's rotation gives the
   second Add vf3. With `U ray` (real memory) or a by-value Add (cse merges the two Adds, origin gets spilled) it can't match.
   After that the Scale earlyclobber / duplicated "j"(s) operand of the older drafts were unnecessary (removed, still MATCH).
Reload facts checked against gcc-2.95.3 reload1.c: find_reload_regs picks per insn the first reg of `potential_reload_regs`
(unused call-used regs ascending, then by use count); choose_reload_regs may only use that insn's `used_spill_regs`
(reload_reg_unavailable = complement) and rotates over them from `last_spill_reg`; sched1 `adjust_priority` halves the priority of
insns with REG_DEAD notes and boosts "birthing" insns, only when they enter the ready list (initially-ready insns keep theirs).

## func_001A3D00 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001A3D00.c), plain C, no asm, no pins
Start: lane5.v2 (aligned 26, with an empty volatile "m"/"memory" asm barrier).
1. **The barrier is not needed for the re-read.** The target's second `lw a1,0(t2)` exists because `w` (the only register holding the
   loaded word) is overwritten by the low-bit `and` before `s->free[i] &= ~w`: cse then has no register for the MEM, and reload_cse can't
   reuse one either. Any variant that keeps the old value in some pseudo (a separate `b = w & -w`, an inline `LowBit(x)` param copy,
   `tmp = mem` from expand_binop's force_reg) loses the re-read (cse or reload_cse turns it into `daddu`).
2. **n=v0 / slot=v1 / address=a0 (local-alloc order): the two stores must be AFTER the loop** (`break;` then
   `s->objects[slot] = o; o->slot = slot;`). After LOOP_END their refs are not doubled, so the address pair drops from 60000 to 20000 and
   n (29000) is allocated first. With the stores after the loop, `slot = cond ? n+16 : n` gets merged by cse with the +8 step; the
   `if (w & 0xFFFF0000U) slot = n + 16; else slot = n;` form keeps the target's movn/movn/movn/movn/movz chain (aligned 2).
3. `s->objects[slot]` (array member) gives `addu a0,s1,a0` (s first); `(char*)s + slot*4 + 0x4154` gives the swapped addu.
4. **`and t1,v1,t1` (neg first): expand_binop swaps commutative operands so that the operand equal to the target comes first, so
   `w &= -w` / `w = -w & w` always give (and w neg).** The target needs `bit = -w & w; w = bit;` (and into a temp, then copy) with w
   remaining cse's canonical register, so the copy target w survives and combine folds the temp into `(set w (and neg w))`. cse's
   make_regs_eqv makes the NEW reg (w) canonical only if it lives outside the current cse block and its last use is later: hence
   `unsigned int w = 0` (any reference to w before the loop works: `for (i = 0, w = 0; ...)` or a function-scope init also MATCH).
General lessons: (a) the order of commutative operands in the final asm is decided at expand time (expand_binop target swap) and
survives unless combine re-canonicalizes; (b) an initialiser that is dead can still change cse's canonical-register choice
(REGNO_FIRST_UID is taken before cse); (c) code moved past LOOP_END has half the ref weight in local-alloc priorities.

## func_001E5FC8 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001E5FC8.c), no pins, no "memory" clobbers, no allocation-only operands
Start: lane7.v6 (aligned 30: unions for start/end/a/b + "memory" clobbers on Dir/Point).
1. **start/end/a/b are plain qword locals** (spilled pseudos: live across the call in the loop, refs < 4*calls). Reload stores them to
   0x20..0x50 (slots in pseudo-number = declaration order start,end,a,b) and loads them with `lq aN,slot` straight into the argument
   registers; the "memory" clobbers are no longer needed (30 -> 26).
2. **The four transforms in ARGUMENT order (start, a, end, b)** (26 -> 16, all VU registers right). The target's emitted order b, end,
   start, a is sched1's `rank_for_schedule` reg-weight tie-break (sets - deaths, from the REG_DEAD notes before scheduling; lower
   first): the last Dir kills m0..m2 + its vector (-3), the last Point kills m3 + its vector (-1), the other two 0 (then luid). All four
   asms become ready together when m0 arrives, so source order alone decides the emitted order through these weights.
3. `duration = p->times[i + 1] - p->times[i]` (no t0): array indexing gives `addu a0,a0,v0` (i*4 first) and evaluating time[1] first
   puts the address death on the time[0] load, which then wins the reg-weight tie (target loads time[0] first) (16 -> 15).
4. `struct M4 *m = &p->mat;` inside the block after the early return: its addiu then takes an ALU issue slot in block 1, which pushes
   the `sll i,2` after the address addus (shares a0 with p->in like the target) (15 -> 3).
5. `p->lengths[j] = p->lengths[j - 1] + ...` instead of a `float *len` cursor: the s0 pointer becomes a loop.c giv whose init lands
   after j=0 and the gcse-hoisted &cur in the preheader (3 -> MATCH).
Lesson: in a block of independent non-volatile asm statements, the emitted order is decided by sched1's register-weight tie-break
(deaths per insn), so reorder the source statements to move the last uses of shared inputs rather than adding barriers.

## func_001D09A0 - MATCHED (src/hand/func_001D09A0.cpp; one pin, one allocation-only operand, one volatile read)
Start: w2lane7.v3, aligned 32. Steps, each found by reading the dumps (gcc 2.95.3 sources of haifa-sched.c, local-alloc.c, global.c,
gcse.c, lcm.c, regmove.c, combine.c were fetched from the gcc-mirror tag releases/gcc-2.95.3 for this):
1. 32 -> 13: one float variable `g` for the dot product at the top AND for the second length readout in the tail (`g = GetX(len)`).
   The target has `$f5` in both places: one pseudo, global, conflicting with f0-f4 in the tail block. As a plain local the dot was
   tied to its last multiply by local-alloc (combine_regs ties an output to a dying local input; a global input cannot be tied).
2. 13 -> 3: the length `len` (vsqrt result) must stay VU class; with two "r" readouts (compare + tail GetX) and two "j" uses the
   regclass costs tie (TI_REGS) and it lands in a GPR. One allocation-only duplicate `"j"(l)` on the vdiv asm breaks the tie.
   Both readouts must be `mtc1 "r"(qword)` asms so the qmfc2.ni is a reload (target $v1 for the compare: reload picks the lowest free
   GPR after global-alloc, $v0 being the fallback pointer).
3. 3 -> 0: the remaining diff was `lui v1,0x2A` (fallback high part) in $v0 and the qmtc2/li.s order. Cause, traced through
   sched1: the high-part pseudo gets $v1 only if its life overlaps the $v0 pseudo holding the loaded vector, i.e. sched1 must issue it
   between that `lq` and the qmtc2. Our qmtc2 was an asm; haifa's insn_cost makes every dependence INTO an asm cost 1 (LINK_COST_FREE),
   so the qmtc2 was ready the cycle after the lq and took the slot. In the target the qmtc2 is a real movti with the lq's latency 2
   (that is also why sched2 puts `li.s 1e-4` between lq and qmtc2). gcc only emits such a move from a hard register: so
   `register qword g asm("$2") = *(volatile qword *)pv; qword v = g;` (the volatile stops combine from folding load+copy into lqc2;
   combine runs recog with volatile MEMs disallowed).
Other facts found on the way (all reusable):
- haifa-sched 2.95 "birthing" boost: when an insn becomes ready and its SET dest is live at the END of the block (bb_live_regs from
  find_pre_sched_live) and REG_N_SETS==1, its priority becomes max_priority = MAX(prio(ready[0]), prio(just scheduled insn)) - after a
  call that is the call's priority. gcse PRE copies inserted at a block end are such insns and steal an issue slot right after a call.
- 2.95 LCM (lcm.c) only uses ANTLOC/TRANSP and LATEIN = DELAYIN for all but the last block, so pre_insert_copies never fires: a global
  used before an if/else and after the join always gets a PRE copy inserted at the END of the first block.
- Issue loop: newly ready insns are written into the slot just vacated and are not issued in the same cycle; issue_rate 2 on the EE.
- regmove inserts a `dst = src` copy before an asm with a matching ("0") operand when the classes differ or src does not die there;
  otherwise it renames the source's setter (so an empty "=j"/"0" asm on a load becomes an lqc2).
- The same NormalizeSafe idiom (fallback pointer D_002A2DA0, eps 1e-4, length returned) is in matched func_00180668.
Dead ends (do not retry): ToVF asm with eps live into the else block via an extra "f"(eps) (aligned 1, sched2 order still wrong);
empty tied asm `"=j"(v):"0"(g)` with 1-3 extra "r"(g) (g goes VU, qmfc2 reloads); plain/union/VU_VECTOR copies (lqc2); pin without
volatile (combine folds to lqc2); volatile without pin (lqc2).

   live length of any pseudo whose first set in insn order has an invariant REG_EQUAL (local-alloc.c:874), even when later sets kill
   the equivalence. Fix without a pin: guard `if (i < count)` instead of `if (count > 0)` (one more ref of i).

   the origin through `static inline const qword &Origin(STATE *s)` makes that load a non-struct MEM, so it depends on the store.

## Reusable lessons (wave 3 lane 6)
1. Read the gcc 2.95.3 source when a dump makes no sense (raw files from github.com/gcc-mirror/gcc tag releases/gcc-2.95.3/gcc/*.c).
   Facts used here: insn_cost gives every dependence into an asm cost 1, so an asm qmtc2/qmfc2 never waits for a load's latency;
   update_equiv_regs doubles the live length of a pseudo whose first set has an invariant REG_EQUAL (cse1 adds one to every
   `x = const`), halving its global-alloc priority; fixed_scalar_and_varying_struct_p makes a fixed scalar local independent of
   struct-member loads, so a load through a const reference (non-struct MEM) is what orders a store before it.
2. A real for/while loop instead of a goto loop, and one counter variable per loop, are cheap natural ways to move s-registers.
3. One variable reused for two unrelated floats (dot product, later length) is a natural way to get one global pseudo.

## Reusable lessons (w3lane7)
1. FPU one-liners as asm (min.s, abs.s) distort sched1 priorities for whole blocks; write `b < a ? b : a` / __builtin_fabsf.
2. `"rV"` on a qmtc2 input word read from memory: the lw becomes a reload next to the qmtc2 (reload reg may equal the dying base reg).
3. gcse PRE reaching regs are numbered in bucket order with hash(plus (reg r) (const_int c)) = (13258 + r + c) mod S,
   S = (insns/2)|1; compute the S values that give the wanted spill-slot order, then move the insn count with VV-vs-qword helper params.
4. la.py reproduces VU local-alloc in call-crossing blocks too (caller-save qtys are allocated in the same priority pass); multi-output
   asms need their output/dying-input tie added by hand (la3.py).

  interleaved with the two asms. An inline GDistPlane reuses ONE frame for both calls (inline frames are freed per call,
  even inside one expression), so it can never give four distinct slots.

  `plane`, so their spill slots come last (0x80/0x90) as in the target.

  cse's "copy after set" swap otherwise makes the longer-lived normalOut the asm target; target has normalIn stored
  directly (sqc2) and normalOut through qmfc2/sq (reload inheritance of the spilled copy).
- `gOut = (gOut < t) ? gOut : t; gIn = (t < gIn) ? gIn : t;` give the target's min.s/max.s operand order.

## Lessons
1. Rewrite fresh in the style of the matched siblings / Lilly's ports before analysing old drafts: func_001C2EF0 went 52 -> 17 -> MATCH,
   func_001C55E0 73 -> 52 and func_001BC688 89 -> 78 on the first natural drafts.
2. Stack-slot order is a strong source oracle: declared objects in declaration order, then purge_addressof objects (address passed
   to a call), then spills in regno order (ADDRESSOF renumbers a variable when its address is first taken), then caller-save slots.
3. A spilled pseudo shows as `lw vX,slot; op vY,..,vX` (reload register != result register); a `qmfc2 GPR,vf; sq GPR,slot` right after
   `sqc2 vf,slot2` is reload inheritance of a spilled copy; asm outputs tie to their first dying input.

(intermediate aligned-4 draft kept as build/hand_wip/func_001A4258.w3lane9.v1.cpp)
Was 72 (w2lane5.v1, mixed volatile flags). Fresh rewrite; each step below was measured:
1. `ToVF(pos)` (qmtc2 asm from the qword param) inside EACH loop body: loop.c hoists it to each preheader (target qmtc2 a0,vf3 / a0,vf10),
   pos stays in a0. A param used directly by "j" asms is VU-class and gets one qmtc2 at entry instead.
2. `vadda.xyzw $ACC, %1, $vf0` (Lilly's operand order): ps2eeas prints/encodes it as `vadda ACC, vf0, vfN` like the target.
3. VV wrapper (`struct VV { qword q; VV(const qword &); operator qword(); }`) as the helpers' return type: the struct copies
   survive until loop.c (loop 2: 86 -> 218 real insns), so 0.0f is no longer hoisted (life 1 * savings 1 * 190 < insns) while 1.0f still is.
   C++ temporaries of VV inside an `if` condition turn the compare into a setcc: compute the float into a variable first.
4. `sqrt.s` helper `__asm__ __volatile__` (as most matched files have it): the barrier orders VU_FLOAT(s)/(root)/(ra)/(rb) after it, which
   gives the target's $v0 transfer chain (len, s, root, ra, rb, dist, num, den all through v0). `float root = SqrtF(1 - s*s);` must be its
   own statement BEFORE `VecCombo(VU_FLOAT(s), u, VU_FLOAT(root), perp)` (else VU_FLOAT(s) is in front of the barrier). 75 -> 52.
5. `Length(v) = Sqrt(Dot(v, v))` (two-operand dot with the same vector) instead of a one-operand DotSelf: raises the axis/u/n tied quantity
   above the dot temp in local-alloc -> axis vf1, dot vf2, one vf3 as in the target. 52 -> 12.
6. Tail order `ba; num = Dot(pa, ba); den = Dot(ba, ba); sDist = GFromVf(Dot(pa, n)); t = GFromVf(num) / GFromVf(den);` (pa dies at the
   dist dot, ba at den -> INSN_REG_WEIGHT makes sched1 issue den before num). 12 -> 7.
7. ONE function-scope `float sDist` shared by both loops: it becomes a global pseudo, allocated after the locals, so loop 1's
   r/sum get f0 and the distance f1 (and loop 2's dist f1). 7 -> 4.
8. The last 4: float extraction by `asm("mtc1 %1,%0" : "=f"(g) : "r"(qword))` (GFromQw: the qmfc2 becomes a reload movti on the
   VU0 unit, the mtc1 an asm with cost 1 and no unit) for every assignment of sDist (both loops) and for num; len and den keep the
   qmfc2 "=r" float asm (GFromVf). Loop 1: the shorter chain (cost 9 = lwc1's) lets the class tie-break put lwc1 before vsub. Tail:
   the vu0-unit qmfc2 of num blocks the [memory vu0] dead store for one cycle -> qmfc2 num, mtc1 num, sqc2 as in the target. MATCH.
Facts learned (sched/alloc, all verified in the -da -fsched-verbose-5 dumps):
- A dependence INTO an asm insn is cost-free after a load (lqc2 -> asm ready the next cycle, class 1 in rank_for_schedule), asm -> asm
  costs 1. Ties then go to the insn independent of the last scheduled one, then to more dependents, then LUID.
- After reload, (plus a2 16) has no known alias base (arg-register ADDRESS is VOIDmode and is not used under PLUS), so offset loads get
  memory deps on the dead stack stores while 0(a2) does not: that changes "dependents" counts in sched2 ties.
- INSN_REG_WEIGHT (sets - REG_DEAD - REG_UNUSED) breaks sched1 priority ties before LUID: which dot kills pa/ba decides their order.
- Multi-output asms (Dot: r + one) count every input twice in REG_N_REFS (each SET has its own ASM_OPERANDS): that is why
  Dot(v, v) raises v's local-alloc priority so much.
